# P2 + P3 中英桥（合并版）—— 拆解 + 整段，每卡 P2→P3

> **这份文件干什么**：把「英式中文/直译预备稿」法用在口语。每张卡先 P2、紧跟它的 P3 follow-ups；每个答案都给两样：
> - **① 拆解（英式中文珠子 → floor 英文）**：卡住时的恢复路径——中文念头 → 英式中文调频（英语语序+口语降级）→ 逐块翻出 floor 英文。
> - **② 整段**：把 floor 英文连起来的完整答案，用来**整段 shadow / 盲录**、找语流。
>
> **P2 vs P3 的英文来源（务必知道）**：
> - **P2 整段 = 你的实产语料**（逆自 [[p2_answers_tagged.md]]，一字未改）→ 可整篇背。
> - **P3 整段 = 按 function-frame + floor 降级词生成的示范**（除少数如 tall buildings=你真语料）→ 定位是**反射训练**（听 stem→function→frame→英式中文），不是背 ~300 条。
>
> **固定模板真源（本文件只加英式中文层，原文件不动）**：P2 glue [[cue_driven_p2.md]] · P3 frame [[function_driven_p3.md]] · 语料 [[p2_answers_tagged.md]]/[[p3_answers_tagged.md]] · 题库 [[question_bank.md]]。
>
> **英式中文 3 动作**：① 修饰甩后面(那些…的/[去]…) ② 让"事"做主语((这件事)) ③ dummy-there/make(有…=there's / 让…=make)。目标口语 floor 大白话。
>
> **怎么用**：P2 → 盖英文读英式中文盲录、再整段 shadow；P3 → 听 stem 先 cold 说、对 function 判定和脊、卡住看珠子怎么降级。

---


## 新01 · A tall building you like or dislike — IFS in Chengdu　(~118s)

**①拆解 · 英式中文珠子 → floor 英文**

| 拍/模式 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| [HOOK] | 我想聊的那栋楼 是 成都的 IFS。 | OK so the building I'd like to talk about is IFS in Chengdu. |
| [点名 · appositive] | 它基本上是一个很高的购物商场，一个最大的之一（城里的）。 | It's basically a really tall shopping mall, one of the biggest in the city. |
| [点名 · dash-unpack] | 里面有很多东西 —— 商店、餐厅、那一类的东西 ——，所以它主要是一个地方，[用来]购物和出去吃饭。 | There's a lot inside — shops, restaurants, that kind of thing — so it's mainly a place for shopping and eating out. |
| [点名 · rel-where] | 它就在成都的正中间，（那儿）真的很容易到达。 | It's right in the middle of Chengdu, where it's really easy to get to. |
| [平移] | 它又高又闪，所以有很多可看的。 | And it's really tall and shiny, so there's a lot to look at. |
| [平移 · like] | 你有这么一个大熊猫雕像，就像一只巨大的熊猫，[正]爬着外墙。 | You've got this big panda statue, like a giant panda climbing up the outside wall. |
| [平移 · like] | 人们总是停下来，[去]拍它的照片。 | People always stop to take photos of it. |
| [平移] | 除此之外，如果你上到顶上，你会得到一个不错的视野，（整座城市的）。 | On top of that, if you go up top, you get a good view of the whole city. |
| [CORE] | 但我爱它的那一点 是 它让成都感觉现代。 | But the thing I love about it is it makes Chengdu feel modern. |
| [EXPLAIN=LAND] | 因为成都是个老城市、有很多历史的，所以一栋又高又闪的楼、就在正中间，给你一种不错的新旧混搭。 | Because Chengdu is an old city with a lot of history, so a tall shiny building right in the middle gives you a nice mix of old and new. |
| [EXPLAIN=LAND] | 所以对我来说，每当朋友来玩，它是最早的那些地方之一，（我带他们去的）。 | So for me, whenever friends come to visit, it's one of the first places I take them. |

**②整段（连起来 shadow）**

> OK so the building I'd like to talk about is IFS in Chengdu. It's basically a really tall shopping mall, one of the biggest in the city. There's a lot inside — shops, restaurants, that kind of thing — so it's mainly a place for shopping and eating out. It's right in the middle of Chengdu, where it's really easy to get to. And it's really tall and shiny, so there's a lot to look at. You've got this big panda statue, like a giant panda climbing up the outside wall. People always stop to take photos of it. On top of that, if you go up top, you get a good view of the whole city. But the thing I love about it is it makes Chengdu feel modern. Because Chengdu is an old city with a lot of history, so a tall shiny building right in the middle gives you a nice mix of old and new. So for me, whenever friends come to visit, it's one of the first places I take them.

**③ P3 follow-ups**

**Q: Are there many tall buildings in your country?** → [FREQUENCY]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| STANCE | 哦，一大堆呢。 | Oh, tons of them. |
| e.g. | 在大城市、比如成都或上海，新的摩天楼在不断地拔起。 | In big cities like Chengdu or Shanghai, new skyscrapers are going up constantly. |
| because | 我会说，这主要是因为(because of)那庞大的人口，所以往上盖 真的是唯一明智的方式、[去]把每个人塞下。 | I'd say it's mainly because of the huge population, so building upwards is really the only sensible way to fit everyone in. |

> Oh, tons of them. In big cities like Chengdu or Shanghai, new skyscrapers are going up constantly. I'd say it's mainly because of the huge population, so building upwards is really the only sensible way to fit everyone in.

**Q: What are the differences between those tall buildings in your country?** → [COMPARE]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| STANCE | 它们其实挺不一样的，我觉得。 | They're quite different, I reckon. |
| 咬差异 | 老一点的那些 往往 就是普通的公寓楼，而新的那些 试着 弄得又时髦又有设计感。 | The older ones tend to be just plain apartment blocks, whereas the newer ones try to look stylish and designed. |
| e.g. | 比如成都的 IFS 和环球中心——玻璃幕墙、造型奇特。 | Like IFS and Global Center in Chengdu — glass walls and weird shapes. |
| LAND | 所以说到底，真正的区别 就在于年代、还有它们在外观上花了多少钱。 | So it's really about the age and how much they spent on the look. |

> They're quite different, I reckon. The older ones tend to be just plain apartment blocks, whereas the newer ones try to look stylish and designed. Like IFS and Global Center in Chengdu — glass walls and weird shapes. So it's really about the age and how much they spent on the look.

**Q: Why are different places laid out and designed differently?** → [REASON]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| STANCE | 主要是因为需求不一样，我会说。 | Mainly because the needs are different, I'd say. |
| because | 因为每个地方 有 不同的人、[做]不同的事情，所以 设计 得配合 人们在那里怎么生活。 | because every place has different people doing different things, so the design has to match how people live there. |
| e.g. | 比如，商业区 需要 高的办公楼，而住宅区 需要 安静、低一点的房子。 | For instance, a business area needs tall office towers, whereas a residential area needs quiet, lower houses. |
| LAND | 话虽如此，气候 也影响 很多——热的地方 盖 得不一样。 | Having said that, the climate matters a lot too — hot places build differently. |

> Mainly because the needs are different, I'd say. Because every place has different people doing different things, so the design has to match how people live there. For instance, a business area needs tall office towers, whereas a residential area needs quiet, lower houses. Having said that, the climate matters a lot too — hot places build differently.

**Q: What are the advantages of living in tall buildings?** → [ENUMERATE]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| STANCE | 其实挺多的。 | Quite a few, actually. |
| 命名最大 | 我觉得最大的一个 就是那个景色——你 住得越高，看 得越远。 | The biggest one, I'd say, is the view — the higher you live, the further you can see. |
| e.g. | 而且通常 有 更好的安保、还有电梯，所以 对老人 也方便。 | And there's usually better security and lifts, so it's easy for old people too. |
| LAND | 话虽如此，如果电梯坏了，就挺麻烦的。 | Having said that, if the lift breaks down, it's a real pain. |

> Quite a few, actually. The biggest one, I'd say, is the view — the higher you live, the further you can see. And there's usually better security and lifts, so it's easy for old people too. Having said that, if the lift breaks down, it's a real pain.

**Q: Why do some people like to remodel and decorate their homes themselves?** → [REASON]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| STANCE | 我会说，主要是为了省钱。 | Mainly to save money, I'd say. |
| because | 因为请人 做 挺贵的，所以 自己动手 让 他们 省 一大笔。 | because paying someone is expensive, so doing it themselves saves them a lot. |
| e.g. | 而且，自己弄 感觉挺好的、当它成功的时候——那房子 更像 他们自己的了。 | And it feels good when it works — the home feels more like their own. |
| LAND | 话虽如此，有些人 就是 享受 那个过程、把它当爱好。 | Having said that, some people just enjoy the process as a hobby. |

> Mainly to save money, I'd say. Because paying someone is expensive, so doing it themselves saves them a lot. And it feels good when it works — the home feels more like their own. Having said that, some people just enjoy the process as a hobby.

**Q: What kind of interior design style do most people like?** → [ENUMERATE]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| STANCE | 我觉得最主流的 就是那种 简约风。 | The most popular one, I'd say, is the simple, minimal style. |
| because | 因为现在大多数人 想要 一个 干净、不乱的空间，所以 他们 保持 颜色淡、家具少。 | because most people these days want a clean, uncluttered space, so they keep the colours light and the furniture simple. |
| e.g. | 比如，白墙、木地板、还有 就 几件家具。 | Like white walls, wooden floors, and just a few pieces of furniture. |
| LAND | 话虽如此，年轻人 现在 开始 喜欢 更有个性、更大胆的颜色了。 | Having said that, younger people are starting to like bolder colours now. |

> The most popular one, I'd say, is the simple, minimal style. Because most people these days want a clean, uncluttered space, so they keep the colours light and the furniture simple. Like white walls, wooden floors, and just a few pieces of furniture. Having said that, younger people are starting to like bolder colours now.

## 新02 · An interesting video — SpaceX catching Starship　(~120s)

**①拆解 · 英式中文珠子 → floor 英文**

| 拍/模式 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| [HOOK] | 我想聊的那个视频 是 一个（我前阵子在手机上看的）。 | So the video I would like to talk about is one I watched on my phone a while ago. |
| [NARRATE-lite] | 我觉得它是在 YouTube 上，是 SpaceX 在发射并接住他们的星舰火箭。 | I think it was on YouTube, and it was SpaceX launching and catching their Starship rocket. |
| [CORE] | 我爱它的那一点 是 那个着陆的部分——它简直太震撼了。 | The thing I love about it is the landing part — it was just mind-blowing. |
| [PLOT] | 它基本上是关于 那个最大、最强的火箭（有史以来造出来的）。 | It is basically about the biggest, most powerful rocket ever built. |
| [PLOT] | 它从发射开始，然后，不是降落在地面上，两只巨大的手臂在半空中接住它。 | It starts with the launch, and then, instead of landing on the ground, two giant arms catch it in mid-air. |
| [PLOT] | 而最酷的部分 是 人们真的管它叫筷子。 | And the cool part is people actually call it the chopsticks. |
| [WISH-BEAT] | 我看它 是因为 我一直都喜欢科幻和太空这类东西，所以我可以看到真的火箭动真格地做（这件事）。 | I watched it because I have always liked sci-fi and space stuff, so I could see real rockets doing it for real. |
| [EXPLAIN=LAND] | 而它打动我的原因 是 有某种真的很美的东西，关于 人类伸手去够星星（这件事）。 | And the reason it got me is there is something really beautiful about humans reaching for the stars. |
| [EXPLAIN=LAND] | 因为我们现在真的在做这件事，不只是在电影里，所以它每次还是让我起鸡皮疙瘩。 | Because we are actually doing it now, not just in movies, so it still gives me goosebumps every time. |
| [EXPLAIN=LAND] | 所以对我来说，我已经看过那个视频好几次了，而它还是让我惊叹。 | So for me, I have watched that video a few times and it still amazes me. |

**②整段（连起来 shadow）**

> So the video I would like to talk about is one I watched on my phone a while ago. I think it was on YouTube, and it was SpaceX launching and catching their Starship rocket. The thing I love about it is the landing part — it was just mind-blowing. It is basically about the biggest, most powerful rocket ever built. It starts with the launch, and then, instead of landing on the ground, two giant arms catch it in mid-air. And the cool part is people actually call it the chopsticks. I watched it because I have always liked sci-fi and space stuff, so I could see real rockets doing it for real. And the reason it got me is there is something really beautiful about humans reaching for the stars. Because we are actually doing it now, not just in movies, so it still gives me goosebumps every time. So for me, I have watched that video a few times and it still amazes me.

**③ P3 follow-ups**

**Q: What kind of videos do people in your country like to watch?** → [ENUMERATE]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| STANCE | 挺多种的，不过最大的那种，我会说，是短视频。 | Quite a few, but the biggest one's short videos, I'd say. |
| e.g. | 比如抖音上那些——几十秒的搞笑片段，或者做饭教程。 | Like the ones on Douyin — those thirty-second funny clips or cooking tutorials. |
| because | 因为大家通勤的时候刷，所以短的、容易看的东西最合适。 | because people watch them while commuting, so short, easy stuff fits best. |

> Quite a few, but the biggest one's short videos, I'd say. Like the ones on Douyin — those thirty-second funny clips or cooking tutorials. Because people watch them while commuting, so short, easy stuff fits best.

**Q: Which is more helpful, watching videos or reading books?** → [COMPARE]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| STANCE | 它们各有各的帮助，我觉得。 | They each help in different ways, I reckon. |
| 咬差异 | 视频往往更快、给你看真实的画面，而书让(make)你更深地思考。 | Videos tend to be faster and show you real footage, whereas books make you think more deeply. |
| e.g. | 比如，学修车，看视频比读文字有用多了。 | For example, learning to fix a car, watching a video is way more useful than reading about it. |
| LAND | 所以它真的取决于你想学什么。 | So it really depends on what you're trying to learn. |

> They each help in different ways, I reckon. Videos tend to be faster and show you real footage, whereas books make you think more deeply. For example, learning to fix a car, watching a video is way more useful than reading about it. So it really depends on what you're trying to learn.

**Q: What skills can people learn from watching videos?** → [ENUMERATE]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| STANCE | 一大堆呢，其实。 | Loads, actually. |
| e.g. | 最实用的那些，我会说，是动手的技能——比如做饭、修东西，或者剪辑视频。 | The most useful ones, I'd say, are hands-on skills — like cooking, fixing things, or editing videos. |
| because | 因为你能看着别人一步一步做，所以比光读说明好跟多了。 | because you can watch someone do it step by step, so it's much easier to follow than just reading instructions. |

> Loads, actually. The most useful ones, I'd say, are hands-on skills — like cooking, fixing things, or editing videos. Because you can watch someone do it step by step, so it's much easier to follow than just reading instructions.

**Q: Are there any differences between the videos that young people and old people like to watch?** → [COMPARE]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| STANCE | 是的，差挺多的，我觉得。 | Yeah, quite a big gap, I reckon. |
| 咬差异 | 年轻人往往看快节奏的搞笑短视频，而老人更喜欢慢一点的东西，比如新闻或者戏曲。 | Young people tend to watch fast, funny short clips, whereas older people prefer slower stuff, like the news or old operas. |
| LAND | 我想这主要是因为(because)他们在不同的年代长大，所以口味就不一样。 | I guess it's mainly because they grew up in a different time, so their taste is just different. |

> Yeah, quite a big gap, I reckon. Young people tend to watch fast, funny short clips, whereas older people prefer slower stuff, like the news or old operas. I guess it's mainly because they grew up in a different time, so their taste is just different.

**Q: Are there any differences between the videos that young men and young women like to watch?** → [COMPARE]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| STANCE | 是的，有一点，不过没那么绝对。 | Yeah, a bit, but it's not a hard line. |
| 咬差异 | 男生往往看更多游戏和体育视频，而女生更喜欢美妆或者 vlog 那类。 | Guys tend to watch more gaming and sports videos, whereas girls go more for makeup or vlog-type stuff. |
| LAND | 话虽如此，很多东西两边都看，比如搞笑片段。 | Having said that, plenty of it overlaps, like funny clips. |

> Yeah, a bit, but it's not a hard line. Guys tend to watch more gaming and sports videos, whereas girls go more for makeup or vlog-type stuff. Having said that, plenty of it overlaps, like funny clips.

**Q: What makes a video go viral online?** → [REASON]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| STANCE | 主要是开头那几秒，我会说。 | Mainly the first few seconds, I'd say. |
| because | 因为大家滑手机滑得很快，所以如果一个视频一下子抓不住你，你就划走了。 | because people scroll so fast, so if a video doesn't grab you straight away, you just swipe past. |
| e.g. | 那些爆火的，通常有个出人意料的转折，或者特别搞笑的东西，让(make)人想转发。 | The ones that blow up usually have a surprising twist or something really funny that makes people want to share it. |

> Mainly the first few seconds, I'd say. Because people scroll so fast, so if a video doesn't grab you straight away, you just swipe past. The ones that blow up usually have a surprising twist or something really funny that makes people want to share it.

## 新03 · A boring place — a small town where relatives live　(~115s)

**①拆解 · 英式中文珠子 → floor 英文**

| 拍/模式 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| [HOOK] | OK 所以我能想到的最无聊的地方 是 一个小镇。 | OK so the most boring place I can think of is a small town. |
| [点名 · rel-where] | 它在乡下，（那儿）我的一些亲戚住着。 | It's out in the countryside, where some of my relatives live. |
| [点名] | 它基本上是一个很小的地方，一点都不特别。 | It's basically a tiny place, nothing special at all. |
| [FACT-DROP] | 我去了那儿，和我的家人，在春节期间，几年前。 | I went there with my family during the Spring Festival, a couple of years ago. |
| [平移 · like] | 而且老实说，当你四处走走的时候，没有多少可看的。 | And honestly, when you walk around, there's not much to see. |
| [平移 · like] | 你有那些基本的东西，像一条主路和几家店。 | You've got the basics, like one main road and a few shops. |
| [平移] | 而差不多就这样了——没有电影院，没有咖啡馆，真的没什么，给年轻人的。 | And that's about it — there was no cinema, no cafes, nothing really for young people. |
| [平移 · with-tag] | 所以我们大部分时间就是坐在房子里，开着电视，聊着天。 | So we spent most of the time just sitting around the house, with the TV on, chatting. |
| [CORE] | 关于它的那一点 是 那儿的生活节奏，对我来说，就是太慢了。 | The thing about it is the pace of life there is just too slow for me. |
| [EXPLAIN=LAND] | 原因是 我习惯了住在城市里，因为在城市里你总能找到点事情做。 | The reason is I'm used to living in a city, because in a city you can always find something to do. |
| [EXPLAIN=LAND · so-tag] | 另外，几乎没有手机信号，所以我甚至没法好好用我的手机。 | Plus there was almost no phone signal, so I couldn't even use my phone properly. |
| [EXPLAIN=LAND] | 所以两天之后我就真的很无聊了。 | So after two days I got really bored. |
| [EXPLAIN=LAND] | 所以对我来说，我就是等不及要回家了。 | So for me, I just couldn't wait to go home. |

**②整段（连起来 shadow）**

> OK so the most boring place I can think of is a small town. It's out in the countryside, where some of my relatives live. It's basically a tiny place, nothing special at all. I went there with my family during the Spring Festival, a couple of years ago. And honestly, when you walk around, there's not much to see. You've got the basics, like one main road and a few shops. And that's about it — there was no cinema, no cafes, nothing really for young people. So we spent most of the time just sitting around the house, with the TV on, chatting. The thing about it is the pace of life there is just too slow for me. The reason is I'm used to living in a city, because in a city you can always find something to do. Plus there was almost no phone signal, so I couldn't even use my phone properly. So after two days I got really bored. So for me, I just couldn't wait to go home.

**③ P3 follow-ups**

**Q: Why do most children think education is boring?** → [REASON]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| STANCE | 我觉得，主要是因为他们只是坐着听。 | Mostly it's because they just sit and listen, I'd say. |
| because | 因为孩子学得最好，当他们能动手做的时候、而不是光被灌，所以坐一整天 真的很闷。 | because kids learn best when they get to do things, not when they're just told stuff, so sitting all day is really dull. |
| e.g. | 比如，一节数学课 常常只是 一个人在讲、你抄笔记。 | For example, a maths class is often just one person talking while you copy notes. |
| LAND | 话虽如此，好老师能 让哪怕枯燥的东西 也变有意思。 | Having said that, a good teacher can make even dull stuff interesting. |

> Mostly it's because they just sit and listen, I'd say. Because kids learn best when they get to do things, not when they're just told stuff, so sitting all day is really dull. For example, a maths class is often just one person talking while you copy notes. Having said that, a good teacher can make even dull stuff interesting.

**Q: Why aren't young people willing to listen to the experiences of older people?** → [REASON]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|

For example, my grandpa talks about finding a job, and it's totally different from today. Having said that, they often do have wisdom worth hearing.

As for solutions, I'd say asking more questions and getting students to do things would help. Having said that, some subjects are just harder to make exciting than others.

## 新04 · A time you got up early — hiking to watch the sunrise　(~120s)

**①拆解 · 英式中文珠子 → floor 英文**

| 拍/模式 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| [HOOK] | 一个时候（想到的） 是 当我很早起床、去爬一座山、看日出。 | One time that comes to mind is when I got up really early to hike up a mountain and watch the sunrise. |
| [HOOK · time anchor] | 那是在一次旅行（几年前的），然后我必须起床、大概4:30，当时天还是漆黑一片。 | It was on a trip a few years ago, and I had to get up around 4:30, when it was still pitch dark. |
| [NARRATE] | 所以我去、和几个朋友。 | So I went with a couple of friends. |
| [NARRATE · which-tag] | 然后我们爬上去、在黑暗里、用我们的手机灯，（那）说实话有点吓人。 | And then we climbed up in the dark with our phone lights, which was honestly a bit scary. |
| [NARRATE] | 所以我们到了山顶、就在太阳升起之前。 | So we made it to the top just before the sun came up. |
| [NARRATE] | 最后我们就坐在那儿，冻僵着、半睡半醒，等着。 | In the end we just sat there, freezing and half-asleep, waiting. |
| [WISH-BEAT] | 我一直很想看太阳升起、从山顶上，因为我听说那景色很棒，所以我起那么早、来赶上它、在天亮之前。 | I'd really wanted to see the sun come up from the top, because I'd heard the view was amazing, so I got up that early to catch it before it got light. |
| [EXPLAIN=LAND] | 而它值得的原因 是 太阳升起的那一刻，整个天空变成了橙色。 | And the reason it was worth it is the moment the sun came up, the whole sky went orange. |
| [EXPLAIN=LAND] | 因为你能看到云、在我们下面，它看起来很棒，我就盯着看。 | Because you could see the clouds below us, it looked amazing, I just stared. |
| [EXPLAIN=LAND] | 所以我一开始感觉又冷又累，但看了那景色之后我感觉充满能量，所以对我来说 它让整个早起真的很值得。 | So I felt cold and tired at first, but after that view I felt full of energy, so for me it made the whole early start really worth it. |

**②整段（连起来 shadow）**

> One time that comes to mind is when I got up really early to hike up a mountain and watch the sunrise. It was on a trip a few years ago, and I had to get up around 4:30, when it was still pitch dark. So I went with a couple of friends. And then we climbed up in the dark with our phone lights, which was honestly a bit scary. So we made it to the top just before the sun came up. In the end we just sat there, freezing and half-asleep, waiting. I'd really wanted to see the sun come up from the top, because I'd heard the view was amazing, so I got up that early to catch it before it got light. And the reason it was worth it is the moment the sun came up, the whole sky went orange. Because you could see the clouds below us, it looked amazing, I just stared. So I felt cold and tired at first, but after that view I felt full of energy, so for me it made the whole early start really worth it.

> Oh yeah, definitely — my wife is one. She gets up at six every morning to work out, even on weekends. I'd say it's mainly because she feels working out in the morning makes her energetic all day.

| e.g. | 还有一些人早起 是为了 有点 属于自己的安静时间，在这一天变忙之前。 | And some people get up early to get a bit of quiet time to themselves before the day gets busy. |

> Mostly it's because of work. Because most people have to commute early to get to work, so they've got no choice but to get up early. And some people get up early to get a bit of quiet time to themselves before the day gets busy.

|---|---|---|

| e.g. | 比如，很多人熬夜 打游戏 或者追剧，就为了那种 独处的时间。 | For example, a lot of people stay up gaming or watching shows, just for that time alone. |

> Not necessarily. Because sometimes arriving too early actually makes the host feel stressed if they're not ready yet. For instance, if you go to someone's for dinner, turning up half an hour early is pretty awkward. Having said that, for a job interview, early is always better.

## 新05 · A person who grows plants — my grandfather　(~120s)

**①拆解 · 英式中文珠子 → floor 英文**

| 拍/模式 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| [HOOK] | 所以我想聊的那个人 是 我爷爷。 | So the person I'd like to talk about is my grandfather. |
| [HOOK · rel-who] | 他八十多岁了，而且他对我真的很特别，因为他就是那一个（那个照顾我的），在我小时候。 | He's in his mid-80s, and he's really special to me, because he's the one who took care of me when I was little. |
| [平移] | 在他不工作以后，种植物（这件事）成了他的爱好，而且他把他的阳台变成了一个小花园。 | After he stopped working, growing plants became his hobby, and he turned his balcony into a small garden. |
| [平移] | 当你看它的时候，有好多可看的。 | When you look at it, there's so much to see. |
| [平移 · like] | 你有蔬菜（那些我们每天吃的），比如西红柿、辣椒和小葱。 | You've got vegetables we eat every day, like tomatoes, hot peppers and spring onions. |
| [平移] | 还有几盆花。 | And a few pots of flowers. |
| [习惯] | 他通常做的事 是，太阳一出来，他就出去给它们浇水，自己做他自己的堆肥，用剩下的果皮，然后检查每一盆。 | What he usually does is, as soon as the sun comes up, he goes out to water them, makes his own compost from leftover fruit peels, and checks on every pot. |
| [习惯] | 他就是每天坚持做，非常有耐心地。 | He just keeps at it every day, very patiently. |
| [ONE-MOMENT] | 我记得有一次他花了一整个下午教我怎么种一朵花，而且他就是慢慢地给我示范怎么小心地处理根。 | I remember one time he spent a whole afternoon teaching me how to plant a flower, and he just showed me slowly how to handle the roots with care. |
| [CORE] | 关于他真正突出的那点 是 他对待每一株植物就像家人一样。 | What really stands out about him is he treats every plant like a family member. |
| [EXPLAIN=LAND] | 原因是，在他不工作以后，它让他平静，也给他一些事做，所以它给他一种真正的平静和目标感。 | The reason is, after he stopped working, it keeps him calm and gives him something to do, so it gives him a true sense of peace and purpose. |
| [EXPLAIN=LAND] | 所以对我来说，说实话，我真的佩服他怎么找到了一个东西（他爱的那个），在他退休时。 | So for me, honestly, I really admire how he found something he loves in his retirement. |

**②整段（连起来 shadow）**

> So the person I'd like to talk about is my grandfather. He's in his mid-80s, and he's really special to me, because he's the one who took care of me when I was little. After he stopped working, growing plants became his hobby, and he turned his balcony into a small garden. When you look at it, there's so much to see. You've got vegetables we eat every day, like tomatoes, hot peppers and spring onions. And a few pots of flowers. What he usually does is, as soon as the sun comes up, he goes out to water them, makes his own compost from leftover fruit peels, and checks on every pot. He just keeps at it every day, very patiently. I remember one time he spent a whole afternoon teaching me how to plant a flower, and he just showed me slowly how to handle the roots with care. What really stands out about him is he treats every plant like a family member. The reason is, after he stopped working, it keeps him calm and gives him something to do, so it gives him a true sense of peace and purpose. So for me, honestly, I really admire how he found something he loves in his retirement.

**③ P3 follow-ups**

| because | 而且它也 让你放松，因为 照料植物(这件事) 是一件很平静的事。 | And it also helps you relax, because looking after plants is a really calming thing. |
| LAND | 话虽如此，它确实 花不少时间。 | Having said that, it does take quite a bit of time. |

> Quite a few, actually. The biggest one's, I'd say, you know exactly what goes into your food — no chemicals. And it also helps you relax, because looking after plants is a really calming thing. Having said that, it does take quite a bit of time.

**Q: Do many people grow vegetables or flowers at home in your country?** → [FREQUENCY]

| because | 我会说，这主要是因为 他们有时间，也 喜欢那种 自己种东西的感觉。 | I'd say it's mainly because they have the time and enjoy the feeling of growing their own stuff. |

> Oh, tons of them. Especially older people, like my grandpa, almost all of them grow something on the balcony. I'd say it's mainly because they have the time and enjoy the feeling of growing their own stuff.

| because | 因为有些植物、比如仙人掌，几乎 不用管，所以任何人都能种活。 | because some plants, like cacti, need almost no care, so anyone can keep them alive. |

| LAND | 所以我会说，入门容易，种好难。 | So I'd say it's easy to start, hard to do well. |

> Honestly, it depends on what you grow. Because some plants, like cacti, need almost no care, so anyone can keep them alive. But vegetables are harder — they need just the right sunlight and water. So I'd say it's easy to start, hard to do well.

**Q: Why do some people like to grow plants?** → [REASON]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |

| because | 一天工作之后，照料植物(这件事) 给你一种 平静的感觉，你的心思 也从压力上移开了。 | After a day's work, looking after plants gives you a calm feeling, and it takes your mind off the stress. |
| e.g. | 比如我外公，他每天早上 第一件事 就是去看他的菜。 | Like my grandpa, the first thing he does every morning is go and check his vegetables. |

> Mainly because it helps them relax, I'd say. After a day's work, looking after plants gives you a calm feeling, and it takes your mind off the stress. Like my grandpa, the first thing he does every morning is go and check his vegetables.

**Q: Why do some people prefer to grow their own fruits and vegetables instead of buying them from the market?** → [REASON]

| e.g. | 而且它也 更省钱——你不用 每次为几根葱 付钱。 | And it also saves money — you don't have to pay for a few spring onions every time. |

> Mostly it's because it's healthier, I'd say. The stuff you grow yourself has no chemicals, so you know exactly what you're eating. And it also saves money — you don't have to pay for a few spring onions every time. Having said that, growing your own can't cover all your needs.

**Q: Do you think students should learn to grow plants?** → [OPINION]

## 新06 · A new law you'd introduce — protect city parks/green spaces (wife angle)　(~120s)

| [FACT-DROP] | 所以 会变的 是，更多公园会留下来，而且这城市会更好住。 | So what would change is, more parks would stay, and the city would be nicer to live in. |

| [WEIGH] | 至于 它会不会受欢迎，大多数普通人会 真的很喜欢它，虽然 一些开发商 可能不会，因为 它限制了 他们能盖的东西。 | As for whether it'd be popular, most ordinary people would really like it, although some developers probably wouldn't, because it limits what they can put up. |

| [HOOK · rel-who] | 我想到这个 是因为我妻子，（她）在城市管理里工作，还 处理这类规定。 | I thought of this because of my wife, who works in city management and deals with these kinds of rules. |
| [习惯 · so-tag] | 她经常看到 绿地被占用，没经过许可，所以 我听说了 这些绿地能 多容易地 消失。 | She often sees green areas being taken over without permission, so I've heard how easily these green areas can disappear. |
| [EXPLAIN=LAND] | 说实话 我真的很在乎这个，因为 绿地 对每个人的健康 有好处。 | Honestly I really care about this, because green spaces are good for everyone's health. |
| [EXPLAIN=LAND · so-tag] | 所以 这城市感觉更干净，而且 人们 有个地方，[去]放松，而对我来说，公园 是值得保护的。 | So the city feels cleaner and people have somewhere to relax, and for me, parks are worth protecting. |

**②整段（连起来 shadow）**

> OK so if I could introduce a new law, it'd be one to better protect the city's parks and green spaces. For me it really comes down to stopping people or companies from taking over public green areas, and stopping them building on them. So what would change is, more parks would stay, and the city would be nicer to live in. People would have more green spots near home, so fewer of these areas would get lost. As for whether it'd be popular, most ordinary people would really like it, although some developers probably wouldn't, because it limits what they can put up. But honestly, for me it's still worth it. I thought of this because of my wife, who works in city management and deals with these kinds of rules. She often sees green areas being taken over without permission, so I've heard how easily these green areas can disappear. Honestly I really care about this, because green spaces are good for everyone's health. So the city feels cleaner and people have somewhere to relax, and for me, parks are worth protecting.

For example, if everyone queues up, no one has to argue about who goes first. At the end of the day, rules are just there to make life easier for everyone.

## 新07 · Childhood friend - Zhangwei　(~115s)

**①拆解 · 英式中文珠子 → floor 英文**

| 拍/模式 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| [H] | 所以我想聊的那个人 是 我童年朋友里的一个，张伟。 | So the person I would like to talk about is one of my childhood friends, Zhangwei. |
| [C] | 关于他真正突出的那点 是 他有多乐于助人和有耐心——他是那种人（大家一卡住就会去找的）。 | What really stands out about him is how helpful and patient he is - he's the kind of guy everyone turns to when they're stuck. |
| [N] | 我们在小学认识的，那时我们大概十二岁，然后我们一直是同学，一直到我们读完高中。 | We met in primary school when we were around twelve, and we stayed classmates right up until we finished high school. |
| [HB] | 我们通常做的（那件事）是 在学校的电脑社团里待着，然后我们会瞎捣鼓，做些小程序。 | What we usually did was hang out in the school computer club, and we'd mess around building little programs. |
| [OM] | 我记得有一次，在一个小组项目上，那个程序崩了，就在展示前。 | I remember one time on a group project, the program crashed just before the presentation. |
| [OM] | 当其他所有人都在慌的时候，他就坐了下来，一行一行地过那些代码。 | While everyone else was freaking out, he just sat down and went through the code line by line. |
| [OM] | 所以在大概二十分钟里，他修好了它——他基本上救了场。 | So in about twenty minutes he fixed it - he basically saved the day. |
| [EL] | 我真的很喜欢他在压力下是怎么保持冷静的，因为他一步一步地检查东西，而不是慌乱。 | I really like how he stays calm under pressure, because he checks things step by step instead of panicking. |
| [EL] | 对他来说，帮忙（这件事）不是一件苦差事——它是一种方式（学习的），所以对我来说，每个人都需要一个朋友，像那样的。 | For him, helping isn't a chore - it's a way of learning, so for me, everyone needs a friend like that. |

**②整段（连起来 shadow）**

> So the person I would like to talk about is one of my childhood friends, Zhangwei. What really stands out about him is how helpful and patient he is - he's the kind of guy everyone turns to when they're stuck. We met in primary school when we were around twelve, and we stayed classmates right up until we finished high school. What we usually did was hang out in the school computer club, and we'd mess around building little programs. I remember one time on a group project, the program crashed just before the presentation. While everyone else was freaking out, he just sat down and went through the code line by line. So in about twenty minutes he fixed it - he basically saved the day. I really like how he stays calm under pressure, because he checks things step by step instead of panicking. For him, helping isn't a chore - it's a way of learning, so for me, everyone needs a friend like that.

| [HOOK · appositive] | OK 那我想聊的那个人 是 某个人（我以前一起共事的），Chen。 | OK so the person I'd like to talk about is someone I used to work with, Chen. |
| [CORE] | 他身上真正突出的那点 是 他把某个东西（他喜欢的）变成了一个真正的生意——他现在开着自己的咖啡店。 | What really stands out about him is he turned something he loves into a real business — he runs his own coffee shop now. |
| [NARRATE-lite · so-tag] | 我们以前在同一家公司工作，所以我就是这么认识他的，而且我们保持了联系，在他离开之后。 | We used to work at the same company, so that's how I met him, and we stayed in touch after he left. |
| [WISH-BEAT] | 他一直都很爱咖啡，所以他想要一份工作（他能真正享受的）。 | He'd always loved coffee, so he wanted a job he could actually enjoy. |
| [WISH-BEAT] | 他从来不喜欢一整天被困在办公桌后面。 | He never liked being stuck behind a desk all day. |
| [NARRATE-lite · dash-unpack] | 所以有一天他就决定放手去干——他辞了工作，开了一家小店。 | So one day he just decided to go for it — he left his job and opened a small shop. |
| [FACT-DROP] | 它是一家小咖啡店，不大，但它总是满满的，而且他有很多常客。 | It's a small coffee shop, not big, but it's always packed, and he's got a lot of regular customers. |
| [EXPLAIN=LAND] | 它能成的主要原因 是 他真的很用心，因为他记得每个人平常点什么，而且这个地方有一种很温馨的感觉。 | The main reason it works is he really puts his heart into it, because he remembers everyone's usual order and the place has a cozy feel. |
| [EXPLAIN=LAND · so-tag] | 所以大家一直回头，而且对我来说，这真的很值得。 | So people keep coming back, and for me, it really paid off. |

**②整段（连起来 shadow）**

> OK so the person I'd like to talk about is someone I used to work with, Chen. What really stands out about him is he turned something he loves into a real business — he runs his own coffee shop now. We used to work at the same company, so that's how I met him, and we stayed in touch after he left. He'd always loved coffee, so he wanted a job he could actually enjoy. He never liked being stuck behind a desk all day. So one day he just decided to go for it — he left his job and opened a small shop. It's a small coffee shop, not big, but it's always packed, and he's got a lot of regular customers. The main reason it works is he really puts his heart into it, because he remembers everyone's usual order and the place has a cozy feel. So people keep coming back, and for me, it really paid off.

**③ P3 follow-ups**

**Q: Why do some people start their own business?** → [REASON]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| STANCE | 主要是为了那份自由，我会说。 | Mainly for the freedom, I'd say. |
| because | 因为(because) 给别人打工 意味着 你得听 你老板的，所以 很多人想 自己当家做主。 | because working for someone else means you have to listen to your boss, so a lot of people want to be their own boss. |
| e.g. | 比如我朋友 Chen，他辞了 他那份稳定工作、[去]开了一家咖啡馆，就因为 他想做 一件他热爱的事。 | For example, my friend Chen, he quit his stable job and opened a coffee shop, just because he wanted to do something he loves. |
| LAND | 话虽如此，有些人 只是单纯想 多赚点钱。 | Having said that, some people just want to make more money. |

> Mainly for the freedom, I'd say. Because working for someone else means you have to listen to your boss, so a lot of people want to be their own boss. For example, my friend Chen, he quit his stable job and opened a coffee shop, just because he wanted to do something he loves. Having said that, some people just want to make more money.

**Q: Should governments provide financial support to start-ups?** → [OPINION]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| STANCE | 是的，绝对该。 | Yes, definitely. |
| because | 因为(because) 新公司 在早期 很难拿到钱，所以 一点政府的帮助 能让它们 活下去。 | because new companies find it hard to get money in the early days, so a bit of government help can keep them alive. |
| e.g. | 比如，给他们减税 或者给点小额贷款，能真的帮到 那些刚起步的人。 | For instance, cutting their taxes or giving small loans can really help people who are just starting out. |
| LAND | 话虽如此，政府该小心点，别把钱浪费在 那些注定要失败的想法上。 | Having said that, the government should be careful not to waste money on ideas that are bound to fail. |

> Yes, definitely. Because new companies find it hard to get money in the early days, so a bit of government help can keep them alive. For instance, cutting their taxes or giving small loans can really help people who are just starting out. Having said that, the government should be careful not to waste money on ideas that are bound to fail.

**Q: Do most people prefer shopping at big stores or small stores?** → [COMPARE]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| STANCE | 大多数人更喜欢大商店，我觉得。 | Most people prefer big stores, I reckon. |
| e.g.（咬差异） | 大商店 什么都有、还更便宜，而小店 更有人情味、但选择少。 | Big stores have everything and are cheaper, whereas small shops are more personal but have less choice. |
| because | 因为(because) 为了 一次买齐 一整周的东西，人们 会去 像沃尔玛那样的大超市。 | because to buy a whole week's stuff in one go, people go to big supermarkets like Walmart. |
| LAND | 所以 这真的 就看 你更在乎 价格、还是那种熟悉的感觉。 | So it really comes down to whether you care more about price or that familiar feeling. |

> Most people prefer big stores, I reckon. Big stores have everything and are cheaper, whereas small shops are more personal but have less choice. Because to buy a whole week's stuff in one go, people go to big supermarkets like Walmart. So it really comes down to whether you care more about price or that familiar feeling.

**Q: What makes a business successful?** → [REASON]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| STANCE | 大多是因为 一个好产品，我会说。 | Mostly it's a good product, I'd say. |
| because | 因为(because) 如果 你卖的东西 是人们真的需要的，他们就会一次次回来，所以 生意 就能稳稳地长起来。 | because if you sell something people really need, they keep coming back, so the business grows steadily. |
| e.g. | 比如 我朋友的咖啡馆，他的咖啡 就是比别人的好，所以 店里一直是满的。 | Like my friend's coffee shop, his coffee is just better than the others, so it's always full of customers. |
| LAND | 话虽如此，运气和时机 也起 很大的作用。 | Having said that, luck and timing also play a big part. |

> Mostly it's a good product, I'd say. Because if you sell something people really need, they keep coming back, so the business grows steadily. Like my friend's coffee shop, his coffee is just better than the others, so it's always full of customers. Having said that, luck and timing also play a big part.

**Q: What makes a business fail?** → [REASON]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| STANCE | 主要是 钱花光了，我会说。 | Mainly running out of money, I'd say. |
| because | 因为(because) 很多新店 花得比赚得多，所以 撑不过几个月 就关门了。 | because a lot of new shops spend more than they earn, so they close down after just a few months. |
| e.g. | 比如，一家餐馆 开在 一个没人经过的地方，就很难拉到客人。 | For instance, a restaurant that opens in a place where nobody passes by finds it hard to bring in customers. |
| LAND | 话虽如此，有时候 就是竞争 太激烈了。 | Having said that, sometimes the competition is just too fierce. |

> Mainly running out of money, I'd say. Because a lot of new shops spend more than they earn, so they close down after just a few months. For instance, a restaurant that opens in a place where nobody passes by finds it hard to bring in customers. Having said that, sometimes the competition is just too fierce.

**Q: Is it easy to set up a new business in your country?** → [OPINION]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| STANCE | 老实说，这要看(depends on) 是什么类型的生意。 | Honestly, it depends on the type of business. |
| because | 因为(because) 开个网店 现在很容易，但 开个实体店 要很多钱 和很多手续。 | because opening an online shop is easy these days, but a physical store needs a lot of money and paperwork. |
| e.g. | 比如，在中国，你几分钟内 就能在网上 注册一家小公司。 | For instance, in China, you can register a small company online in a few minutes. |
| LAND | 话虽如此，真正 把它做起来、赚到钱，是 完全另一回事。 | Having said that, actually making it work and earning money is a whole different thing. |

> Honestly, it depends on the type of business. Because opening an online shop is easy these days, but a physical store needs a lot of money and paperwork. For instance, in China, you can register a small company online in a few minutes. Having said that, actually making it work and earning money is a whole different thing.

## 新10 · A plan you had to change recently — trip changed (son got a fever)　(~110s)

**①拆解 · 英式中文珠子 → floor 英文**

| 拍/模式 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| [HOOK] | OK 那么，一个冒到我脑子里的时候 是 我最近不得不改一个旅行计划的时候。 | OK so one time that comes to mind is when I recently had to change a travel plan. |
| [NARRATE] | 那是在国庆假期期间，然后我们本来要去另一个城市待几天。 | It was over the National Day holiday, and we were going to visit another city for a few days. |
| [NARRATE so-tag] | 但就在我们出发前，我儿子木叶发起烧来，所以我们觉得 那不是个好主意，[去]带着他那样出去旅行。 | But just before we left, my son Muye came down with a fever, so we decided it wasn't a good idea to travel with him like that. |
| [NARRATE] | 所以我们不得不把它取消掉。 | So we had to call it off. |
| [NARRATE] | 所以作为替代，我们就 待在城市周围，然后放轻松。 | So instead we just stayed around the city and took it easy. |
| [NARRATE to-purpose] | 我们逛了几个公园，在一些不错的餐厅吃饭，还让他休息，[来]好起来。 | We visited a few parks, ate at some nice restaurants, and let him rest to get better. |
| [NARRATE] | 一开始我有点失望，因为我真的一直很期待这次旅行。 | At first I was a bit disappointed, because I'd really been looking forward to the trip. |
| [NARRATE] | 但最后 结果还不错，我们还是过了一段放松的时光，然后木叶很快就好了。 | But in the end it turned out fine, we still had a relaxing time, and Muye got better quickly. |
| [EXPLAIN=LAND] | 所以对我来说，一个计划的改变（这件事）并不总是一件坏事。 | So for me, a change of plan isn't always a bad thing. |

**②整段（连起来 shadow）**

> OK so one time that comes to mind is when I recently had to change a travel plan. It was over the National Day holiday, and we were going to visit another city for a few days. But just before we left, my son Muye came down with a fever, so we decided it wasn't a good idea to travel with him like that. So we had to call it off. So instead we just stayed around the city and took it easy. We visited a few parks, ate at some nice restaurants, and let him rest to get better. At first I was a bit disappointed, because I'd really been looking forward to the trip. But in the end it turned out fine, we still had a relaxing time, and Muye got better quickly. So for me, a change of plan isn't always a bad thing.

**③ P3 follow-ups**

**Q: Do people often change their plans?** → [FREQUENCY]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| STANCE | 哦，一直在改。 | Oh, all the time. |
| because | 现在生活变得这么快，所以有个事 冒出来、[去]打乱 你原本安排好的，是很正常的。 | Life moves so fast these days, so it's normal for something to come up and mess up what you'd arranged. |
| LAND | 话虽如此，人们通常还是会坚持 那些大的，比如结婚。 | Having said that, people usually still stick to the big ones, like getting married. |

> Oh, all the time. Life moves so fast these days, so it's normal for something to come up and mess up what you'd arranged. Having said that, people usually still stick to the big ones, like getting married.

**Q: Would you tell others if you change your plan?** → [OPINION]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| STANCE | 老实说，看是谁。 | Honestly, it depends on who. |
| because | 如果这个改变影响到 别人，比如 一个在等我的朋友，那我当然会 让他们知道。 | If the change affects other people, like a friend who's waiting for me, then of course I'll let them know. |
| e.g. | 但如果只是我自己的小事，我一般就懒得 [去]说了。 | But if it's just some small thing of my own, I usually don't bother telling them. |

> Honestly, it depends on who. If the change affects other people, like a friend who's waiting for me, then of course I'll let them know. But if it's just some small thing of my own, I usually don't bother telling them.

**Q: Why do you think parents still make plans for their children nowadays?** → [REASON]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| STANCE | 主要是出于担心吧，我会说。 | Mainly out of worry, I'd say. |
| because | 因为(because)竞争太激烈了，所以家长觉得，如果不早点规划，孩子就会落后。 | because the competition is so fierce, parents feel that if they don't plan early, their kids will fall behind. |
| e.g. | 比如 帮孩子报补习班、挑学校，都是提前 [去]铺好路。 | Like signing kids up for tutoring or picking schools, it's all about paving the way in advance. |
| LAND | 话虽如此，我觉得 有时给孩子一点自己选的空间会更好。 | Having said that, I think sometimes giving kids a bit of room to choose for themselves would be better. |

> Mainly out of worry, I'd say. Because the competition is so fierce, parents feel that if they don't plan early, their kids will fall behind. Like signing kids up for tutoring or picking schools, it's all about paving the way in advance. Having said that, I think sometimes giving kids a bit of room to choose for themselves would be better.

**Q: How does technology help people make plans?** → [ENUMERATE]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| STANCE | 帮了不少呢，说实话。 | Loads, actually. |
| e.g. | 最大的一个，我会说，就是手机上的日历和提醒——我把要做的事全记进去，然后它会 提前戳我一下。 | The biggest one, I'd say, is the calendar and reminders on your phone — I put everything I need to do in there, and it pokes me in advance. |
| LAND | 话虽如此，如果你太依赖它，你就慢慢 不自己动脑子了。 | Having said that, if you rely on it too much, you kind of stop thinking for yourself. |

> Loads, actually. The biggest one, I'd say, is the calendar and reminders on your phone — I put everything I need to do in there, and it pokes me in advance. Having said that, if you rely on it too much, you kind of stop thinking for yourself.

**Q: What kind of plans do people often make?** → [ENUMERATE]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| STANCE | 各种各样吧，说真的。 | All sorts, really. |
| e.g. | 最常见的，我会说，就是 关于工作和旅行 那些——比如 计划一次假期，[去]决定 去哪、待多久。 | The most common ones, I'd say, are the ones about work and travel — like planning a holiday, deciding where to go and how long to stay. |
| LAND | 话虽如此，很多人也会 做些长远的，比如 存钱买房。 | Having said that, a lot of people also make long-term ones, like saving up to buy a house. |

> All sorts, really. The most common ones, I'd say, are the ones about work and travel — like planning a holiday, deciding where to go and how long to stay. Having said that, a lot of people also make long-term ones, like saving up to buy a house.

**Q: Do you think people like the process of making plans more, or the moment of carrying them out?** → [COMPARE]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| STANCE | 我觉得大多数人更喜欢 真正去做的那一刻。 | Most people enjoy actually doing it more, I'd say. |
| 咬差异 | 因为做计划 会让人有点头疼，而 把计划实现 才让人感觉好、当它成了的时候。 | because planning can be a bit of a headache, whereas carrying the plan out feels good when it works. |
| LAND | 所以说到底，关键在于 最后那个回报。 | So it's really about the payoff at the end. |

> Most people enjoy actually doing it more, I'd say. Because planning can be a bit of a headache, whereas carrying the plan out feels good when it works. So it's really about the payoff at the end.

## 新11 · A time you worked in a group (software project at work)　(~112s)

**①拆解 · 英式中文珠子 → floor 英文**

| 拍/模式 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| [HOOK] | 有一次（我想到的那次）是我在一个小组里工作的时候，做一个软件项目，在公司里。 | One time that comes to mind is when I worked in a group on a software project at work. |
| [NARRATE · dash-unpack] | 所以有大概我们四五个人，我的活主要是搞后端 —— 就是那部分（用户看不到的）。 | So there were about four or five of us, and my job was mainly to handle the backend — the part users don't see. |
| [FACT-DROP · like] | 它就是个小团队，就像几个开发和一个设计师，然后我们分活，按每个人擅长什么。 | It was just a small team, like a couple of developers and a designer, and we split the work by what each person was good at. |
| [NARRATE] | 然后截止日期变得很紧，而且除此之外，需求一直在变。 | And then the deadline got really tight, and on top of that the requirements kept changing. |
| [NARRATE · which-tag] | 所以我们开短会，几乎每天，来保持大家步调一致。 | So we had short meetings almost every day to stay on the same page. |
| [NARRATE] | 然后最后我们加班，大概一个星期，来把它做完。 | And in the end we worked overtime for about a week to get it done. |
| [EXPLAIN=LAND] | 那个原因（我们组队干活的）很简单，因为这个项目就是太大了，对一个人来说。 | The reason we worked as a group is simple, because the project was just too big for one person. |
| [EXPLAIN=LAND] | 每个人有不同的技能，所以一起做才说得通。 | Everyone had different skills, so it made sense to do it together. |
| [EXPLAIN=LAND] | 所以对我来说，感觉真的很好，能作为一个团队一起把它搞成。 | So for me, it felt really good to pull it off together as a team. |

**②整段（连起来 shadow）**

> One time that comes to mind is when I worked in a group on a software project at work. So there were about four or five of us, and my job was mainly to handle the backend — the part users don't see. It was just a small team, like a couple of developers and a designer, and we split the work by what each person was good at. And then the deadline got really tight, and on top of that the requirements kept changing. So we had short meetings almost every day to stay on the same page. And in the end we worked overtime for about a week to get it done. The reason we worked as a group is simple, because the project was just too big for one person. Everyone had different skills, so it made sense to do it together. So for me, it felt really good to pull it off together as a team.

**③ P3 follow-ups**

**Q: Why do some people prefer to work by themselves?** → [REASON]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| STANCE | 大多是因为(because of) 掌控感，我会说。 | Mostly it's about control, I'd say. |
| because | 因为 当你独自工作，你不用 等别人，所以你可以 就按自己的方式做事。 | because when you work alone, you don't have to wait for others, so you can do things your own way. |
| e.g. | 而且有些人 没人在旁边讲话(without people talking around them)，就是更专注。 | And some people just focus better without people talking around them. |
| LAND | 话虽如此，那也可能变得挺孤单的。 | Having said that, it can get a bit lonely. |

> Mostly it's about control, I'd say. Because when you work alone, you don't have to wait for others, so you can do things your own way. And some people just focus better without people talking around them. Having said that, it can get a bit lonely.

**Q: What should a leader do to make team members want to follow him or her?** → [ENUMERATE]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| STANCE | 我会说，最大的一点是 信任。 | The biggest one's trust, I'd say. |
| because | 一个好领导 会听大家的(listens to people)，所以每个人都觉得 自己的意见 算数。 | A good leader listens to people, so everyone feels their opinion counts. |
| e.g. | 比如，如果 有个问题(there's a problem)，他们不会 就只怪你——他们会帮你 把它解决掉。 | For instance, if there's a problem, they don't just blame you — they help you fix it. |
| LAND | 所以说，这其实主要是关于 尊重。 | So it's really mostly about respect. |

> The biggest one's trust, I'd say. A good leader listens to people, so everyone feels their opinion counts. For instance, if there's a problem, they don't just blame you — they help you fix it. So it's really mostly about respect.

**Q: Should students learn to do group work?** → [OPINION]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| STANCE | 绝对应该。 | Definitely. |
| because | 因为 在真实的工作里，你几乎从不 完全一个人干，所以这是 一项技能，(是)他们以后会需要的(they'll need later)。 | because in real jobs, you almost never work completely alone, so it's a skill they'll need later. |
| e.g. | 比如，他们学会 怎么分配任务、也学会应付 那些他们不认同的人(people they don't agree with)。 | For example, they learn how to split tasks and deal with people they don't agree with. |
| LAND | 话虽如此，也不该 每一个作业(every single assignment) 都这么搞。 | Having said that, it shouldn't be every single assignment. |

> Definitely. Because in real jobs, you almost never work completely alone, so it's a skill they'll need later. For example, they learn how to split tasks and deal with people they don't agree with. Having said that, it shouldn't be every single assignment.

**Q: What group tasks are there in schools?** → [ENUMERATE]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| STANCE | 其实挺多的。 | Quite a few, actually. |
| name | 我会说，最常见的一种 可能是 小组展示(group presentations)。 | The most common one's probably group presentations, I'd say. |
| e.g. | 还有像 科学项目 那种，(在里面)每个人 做一部分。 | And things like science projects too, where everyone does one part. |
| LAND | 所以说，种类还挺多样的。 | So there's a decent mix, really. |

> Quite a few, actually. The most common one's probably group presentations, I'd say. And things like science projects too, where everyone does one part. So there's a decent mix, really.

**Q: What advantages are there for students experiencing teamwork at school?** → [ENUMERATE]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| STANCE | 我会说，最大的好处是 沟通。 | The biggest one's communication, I'd say. |
| because | 因为 他们学会 怎么解释 自己的想法，好让别人 真的能听懂(so other people actually get them)。 | because they learn how to explain their ideas so other people actually get them. |
| e.g. | 而且他们也学到 耐心，因为 不是每个人 都用同样的速度做事。 | And they also pick up patience, since not everyone works at the same speed. |
| LAND | 所以说，他们对 真实的工作 会更有准备。 | So they come out more ready for real work. |

> The biggest one's communication, I'd say. Because they learn how to explain their ideas so other people actually get them. And they also pick up patience, since not everyone works at the same speed. So they come out more ready for real work.

**Q: How can you tell if a person is a good leader?** → [ENUMERATE]

## 新14 · A food people eat on special occasions (birthday cake — wife bakes)　(~115s)

**①拆解 · 英式中文珠子 → floor 英文**

| 拍/模式 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| [HOOK · which-tag] | 我想聊的那个食物 是 生日蛋糕，人们吃它来庆祝生日。 | The food I'd like to talk about is birthday cake, which people eat to celebrate birthdays. |
| [CORE] | 在我家 它算是件挺大的事，因为我老婆爱烘焙，而且通常自己做。 | In my family it's kind of a big deal, because my wife loves baking and usually makes it herself. |
| [FACT-DROP] | 它是给生日的，而一个好例子 是 那个恐龙蛋糕，她给我们儿子牧野做的，他超级喜欢。 | It's for birthdays, and a good example is the dinosaur cake she made for our son Muye, which he absolutely loved. |
| [习惯 · dash-unpack] | 她通常做的 是 烤一个海绵蛋糕底，给它裹上奶油，然后塑形和装饰它——那次把整个东西变成一只绿色恐龙。 | What she usually does is bake a sponge base, cover it with cream, and then shape and decorate it — that time turning the whole thing into a green dinosaur. |
| [EXPLAIN=LAND] | 至于为什么人们在生日吃它，原因 是 它真的就是关于庆祝，因为你在上面插蜡烛，许个愿，然后把它们吹灭，当大家在唱歌的时候。 | As for why people eat it on birthdays, the reason is it's really about celebrating, because you put candles on it, make a wish, and blow them out while everyone sings. |
| [EXPLAIN=LAND · so-tag] | 所以它是个甜蜜的小时刻，而且它把全家聚到一起。 | So it's a sweet little moment, and it brings the whole family together. |
| [EXPLAIN=LAND] | 所以对我们来说，这蛋糕并不真的是关于食物本身——它更多是关于那份快乐记忆，围绕着它的。 | So for us, the cake isn't really about the food itself — it's more about the happy memory around it. |

**②整段（连起来 shadow）**

> The food I'd like to talk about is birthday cake, which people eat to celebrate birthdays. In my family it's kind of a big deal, because my wife loves baking and usually makes it herself. It's for birthdays, and a good example is the dinosaur cake she made for our son Muye, which he absolutely loved. What she usually does is bake a sponge base, cover it with cream, and then shape and decorate it — that time turning the whole thing into a green dinosaur. As for why people eat it on birthdays, the reason is it's really about celebrating, because you put candles on it, make a wish, and blow them out while everyone sings. So it's a sweet little moment, and it brings the whole family together. So for us, the cake isn't really about the food itself — it's more about the happy memory around it.

**Q: Why are there special foods on special occasions or events?** → [REASON]

> Mainly tradition, I'd say. Because these foods are passed down from generation to generation, so eating them makes the day feel special and different from normal days. For example, we make a cake for birthdays — you wouldn't make a cake on a normal Tuesday. Having said that, I think some people just follow the crowd and don't really think about the meaning behind it.

|---|---|---|
| STANCE | 变化挺大的，是的。 | Massively, yeah. |
| 骨架 | 我们过去 大多吃 自己在家做的、时令的东西，但现在 我们 什么时候都能买到 任何东西。 | We used to mostly eat homemade, seasonal stuff, but now we can buy anything at any time. |
| e.g. | 比如，现在 很多东西 都是加工好的、或者 点外卖。 | For example, these days a lot of it is processed or ordered as takeaway. |
| LAND | 话虽如此，我觉得 有些老菜 正在回归，因为人们 想吃得更健康。 | Having said that, I think some old dishes are coming back because people want to eat healthier. |

> Massively, yeah. We used to mostly eat homemade, seasonal stuff, but now we can buy anything at any time. For example, these days a lot of it is processed or ordered as takeaway. Having said that, I think some old dishes are coming back because people want to eat healthier.

**Q: Do people today prefer eating at home or in a restaurant?** → [COMPARE]

| 咬差异 | 因为 在家做饭 又花时间、又麻烦，而 下馆子 又快又省事。 | because cooking at home takes time and is a hassle, whereas eating out is quick and easy. |

| LAND | 话虽如此，家里做的饭 还是 更便宜、也更健康。 | Having said that, home-cooked food is still cheaper and healthier. |

> More and more people eat out these days, I'd say. Because cooking at home takes time and is a hassle, whereas eating out is quick and easy. For example, a lot of working people just order takeaway after a long day. Having said that, home-cooked food is still cheaper and healthier.

**Q: What are the advantages and disadvantages of AI?** → [TWO-SIDED]

| because | 因为 AI 能瞬间处理 那些枯燥、重复的活儿，比如写代码或者查资料。 | because AI can handle those boring, repetitive jobs in seconds, like writing code or looking things up. |
| LAND | 话虽如此，一个坏处是 人们会太依赖它，然后停止(去)自己思考。 | Having said that, one downside is people rely on it too much and stop thinking for themselves. |

> It's a bit of a mixed bag, honestly. The big plus is it saves us loads of time. Because AI can handle those boring, repetitive jobs in seconds, like writing code or looking things up. Having said that, one downside is people rely on it too much and stop thinking for themselves.

**Q: Do you think people today should learn about AI technology?** → [OPINION]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| STANCE | 绝对的。 | Definitely. |
| because | 因为如今 AI 到处都是，所以如果你不懂它怎么运作，你就会被落下。 | because AI is everywhere these days, so if you don't get how it works, you'll get left behind. |
| e.g. | 比如，就算是简单的工作，现在也期待 人们用这些工具。 | For example, even simple jobs now expect people to use these tools. |
| LAND | 话虽如此，我不觉得每个人都需要 (去)编程——知道怎么用它就够了。 | Having said that, I don't think everyone needs to code — just knowing how to use it is enough. |

> Definitely. Because AI is everywhere these days, so if you don't get how it works, you'll get left behind. For example, even simple jobs now expect people to use these tools. Having said that, I don't think everyone needs to code — just knowing how to use it is enough.

**Q: Should children learn to use AI?** → [OPINION]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| STANCE | 说实话，这取决于(depends on)他们的年龄。 | Honestly, it depends on their age. |
| because | 因为对大点的孩子，AI 是个 很棒的学习工具，(那种)能给他们讲解 难懂东西的。 | because for older kids, AI is a great learning tool that can explain hard stuff to them. |
| LAND | 但对小小孩，我会说先打好基础，别一上来就靠机器。 | But for young ones, I'd say build the basics first, don't lean on a machine straight away. |

> Honestly, it depends on their age. Because for older kids, AI is a great learning tool that can explain hard stuff to them. But for young ones, I'd say build the basics first, don't lean on a machine straight away.

**Q: How can AI help in our lives?** → [ENUMERATE]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| STANCE | 其实有一大堆(方式)。我会说，最大的那个 是省时间。 | Loads of ways, actually. The biggest one's saving time, I'd say. |
| e.g. | 比如，它能帮你 (去)回复邮件、规划你的一天，甚至(去)诊断疾病。 | For instance, it can help you reply to emails, plan your day, or even diagnose illnesses. |
| because | 因为它一次能处理 海量信息，比人快得多。 | because it can process loads of information at once, much faster than a human. |

> Loads of ways, actually. The biggest one's saving time, I'd say. For instance, it can help you reply to emails, plan your day, or even diagnose illnesses. Because it can process loads of information at once, much faster than a human.

**Q: Do you think students are overly reliant on AI?** → [OPINION]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| STANCE | 某种程度上，是的。 | To some extent, yes. |
| because | 因为它太方便了，所以 很多学生就直接 (去)抄答案，而不(去)自己思考。 | because it's so convenient, so a lot of students just copy the answers instead of thinking for themselves. |
| e.g. | 比如写作业时，他们让 AI 全写了，然后自己什么也没学到。 | For example, with homework, they get AI to write the whole thing and learn nothing. |
| LAND | 话虽如此，如果用得聪明，它也能是个很好的老师。 | Having said that, if it's used smartly, it can be a great teacher too. |

> To some extent, yes. Because it's so convenient, so a lot of students just copy the answers instead of thinking for themselves. For example, with homework, they get AI to write the whole thing and learn nothing. Having said that, if it's used smartly, it can be a great teacher too.

**Q: What can teachers do to stop students from relying too much on AI?** → [ENUMERATE]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| STANCE | 其实能做的不少。 | Quite a few things, actually. |
| e.g. | 我会说，最有效的一个 是让 学生在课堂上手写作业。 | The most effective one, I'd say, is making students write in class by hand. |
| because | 因为这样一来 他们就没法(去)靠 AI，所以他们不得不自己动脑。 | because that way they can't lean on AI, so they have to use their own brains. |
| LAND | 话虽如此，我不会完全把它禁掉——教他们怎么正确地用 会更好。 | Having said that, I wouldn't ban it completely — teaching them how to use it properly is better. |

> Quite a few things, actually. The most effective one, I'd say, is making students write in class by hand. Because that way they can't lean on AI, so they have to use their own brains. Having said that, I wouldn't ban it completely — teaching them how to use it properly is better.

| [CORE] | 他身上真正突出的那点 是 他是那种人（很多人都仰望的）。 | What really stands out about him is that he's someone a lot of people look up to. |
| [FACT-DROP · dash-unpack] | 而且你基本上到处都能看到它 —— 在电视上、在社交媒体上、在广告牌上。 | And you see it pretty much everywhere — on TV, on social media, on billboards. |
| [PLOT] | 所以这个广告 基本上是关于 不放弃。 | So the ad is basically about not quitting. |
| [PLOT] | 它开始于 那个运动员深夜一个人训练，一次又一次地失败。 | It starts with the player training alone late at night, failing again and again. |
| [PLOT] | 而酷的那部分 是 他最后成功了，所以那个信息 是 努力会有回报。 | And the cool part is he finally succeeds, so the message is that hard work pays off. |
| [EXPLAIN=LAND] | 我喜欢它的原因 是 它做得真的很好，因为它在很短的时间里做了很多。 | The reason I like it is that it's really well made, because it does a lot in a short time. |
| [EXPLAIN=LAND · which-tag] | 它只有三十秒，（那很短），但它真的让你很有干劲。 | It's only thirty seconds, which is short, but it really gets you motivated. |
| [EXPLAIN=LAND] | 在我内心深处我知道 他们只是在卖鞋，但它对我还是有用。 | In the back of my mind I know they're just selling shoes, but it still works on me. |

**②整段（连起来 shadow）**

> OK so the ad I'd like to talk about is a sportswear ad with a famous person in it, a famous basketball player. What really stands out about him is that he's someone a lot of people look up to. And you see it pretty much everywhere — on TV, on social media, on billboards. So the ad is basically about not quitting. It starts with the player training alone late at night, failing again and again. And the cool part is he finally succeeds, so the message is that hard work pays off. The reason I like it is that it's really well made, because it does a lot in a short time. It's only thirty seconds, which is short, but it really gets you motivated. In the back of my mind I know they're just selling shoes, but it still works on me. So for me, I'd say it's a really clever ad.

| because | 因为 你每天 看到 那么多广告，所以 一个 会粘在你脑子里的 广告 才是 有用的那个。 | because you see so many ads every day, so an ad that sticks in your head is the useful one. |
| e.g. | 比如，一句 简单又朗朗上口的 口号 让 一个牌子 待在 你记忆里。 | For instance, a simple, catchy slogan keeps a brand in your memory. |
| LAND | 话虽如此，产品本身 也得 是好的。 | Having said that, the product itself has to be good too. |

> The biggest one's making it easy to remember, I'd say. Because you see so many ads every day, so an ad that sticks in your head is the useful one. For instance, a simple, catchy slogan keeps a brand in your memory. Having said that, the product itself has to be good too.

**Q: Why are some advertisements boring?** → [REASON]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| STANCE | 我会说，主要是 没有新意。 | Mainly a lack of fresh ideas, I'd say. |
| because | 因为 有些公司 就是 抄 别人做过的东西，所以 广告 看起来 全都一样。 | because some companies just copy what others have done, so the ads all look the same. |
| e.g. | 比如，那些 没完没了的 药品广告——总是 同一个 微笑的医生。 | For instance, those endless medicine ads — always the same smiling doctor. |
| LAND | 话虽如此，一个好点子 能让 哪怕 无聊的产品 也变有趣。 | Having said that, a good idea can make even a boring product fun. |

> Mainly a lack of fresh ideas, I'd say. Because some companies just copy what others have done, so the ads all look the same. For instance, those endless medicine ads — always the same smiling doctor. Having said that, a good idea can make even a boring product fun.

**Q: Is advertising important for a company? Why?** → [OPINION]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| STANCE | 绝对的。 | Definitely. |
| because | 因为 就算 你的产品 很好，如果 没人 知道 它，也 没人 会买。 | because even if your product is great, if nobody knows about it, nobody will buy it. |
| e.g. | 比如，很多 小品牌 一夜爆红，就 因为(because of) 一个 火了的短视频。 | For instance, lots of small brands blow up overnight because of one video that goes viral. |
| LAND | 话虽如此，有时候 人们 互相 推荐 比广告 更管用。 | Having said that, sometimes people recommending it to each other works better than ads. |

> Definitely. Because even if your product is great, if nobody knows about it, nobody will buy it. For instance, lots of small brands blow up overnight because of one video that goes viral. Having said that, sometimes people recommending it to each other works better than ads.

**Q: Which is more effective, online advertising or offline advertising?** → [COMPARE]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| STANCE | 我觉得，它们 挺不一样的。 | They're quite different, I reckon. |
| 咬差异 | 网上广告 倾向于 精准找到你，而 线下的、比如 广告牌，只是 撒给 所有人。 | Online ads tend to target you precisely, whereas offline ones like billboards just spread out to everyone. |
| e.g. | 比如，网上 你 搜过 一双鞋，然后 那双鞋的广告 到处 跟着你。 | For instance, online you search for a pair of shoes, and then the ad for them follows you everywhere. |
| LAND | 所以 这真的 取决于(on) 你想 触达 谁。 | So it's really about who you want to reach. |

> They're quite different, I reckon. Online ads tend to target you precisely, whereas offline ones like billboards just spread out to everyone. For instance, online you search for a pair of shoes, and then the ad for them follows you everywhere. So it's really about who you want to reach.

|---|---|---|

| [HOOK] | 我去过那里几次，然后我每次都爱它。 | I've been there a few times, and I love it every time. |
| [点名 · appositive] | 它是一座城市，在日本那边。 | It's a city over in Japan. |
| [点名 · appositive] | 它很有名，因为它那些古老的寺庙和传统的街道。 | It's famous for its old temples and traditional streets. |
| [平移] | 而当你四处走的时候，有好多可看的。 | And when you walk around, there's so much to see. |
| [平移 · like] | 你有漂亮的古老寺庙，比如那个金阁寺。 | You've got beautiful old temples, like the Golden Pavilion. |
| [习惯] | 我在那里做的 是 到老街区里走走，试很多当地的街边小吃，然后就好好感受这一切。 | What I did there was walk around the old neighbourhoods, try lots of local street food, and just take it all in. |
| [习惯 · rel-who] | 而且你看到人们四处走着，穿着传统服装，（那些人）真的让这地方感觉很古老。 | And you see people walking around in traditional clothes, who really make the place feel old. |
| [EXPLAIN=LAND] | 所以我会推荐它的原因 是 它真的很宁静、充满历史，因为它不是一座吵闹、忙碌的城市。 | So the reason I'd recommend it is that it's really peaceful and full of history, because it's not a loud, busy city. |
| [EXPLAIN=LAND · so-tag] | 没有什么可赶的，所以你可以慢下来，把一切都收进来。 | There's no rush, so you can slow down and take everything in. |
| [EXPLAIN=LAND · so-tag] | 所以对我来说，如果有人喜欢文化和安静的地方，我会叫他们去。 | So for me, if someone likes culture and quiet places, I'd tell them to go. |

**②整段（连起来 shadow）**

> OK so the place I'd like to recommend is Kyoto. I've been there a few times, and I love it every time. It's a city over in Japan. It's famous for its old temples and traditional streets. And when you walk around, there's so much to see. You've got beautiful old temples, like the Golden Pavilion. What I did there was walk around the old neighbourhoods, try lots of local street food, and just take it all in. And you see people walking around in traditional clothes, who really make the place feel old. So the reason I'd recommend it is that it's really peaceful and full of history, because it's not a loud, busy city. There's no rush, so you can slow down and take everything in. So for me, if someone likes culture and quiet places, I'd tell them to go.

| [平移 · with-tag] | 你有这栋漂亮的老木头房子，带着草席地板。 | You've got this beautiful old wooden house, with straw-mat floors. |
| [平移 · with-tag] | 而有一个小花园，在后面。 | And there's a small garden in the back. |

It was really quiet, so in the morning you could hear the birds. The reason I love visiting is it feels like a little escape from city life, because it's so calm and relaxing. So for me, it's just a totally different pace from normal life. But honestly, I wouldn't want to actually live there. **①拆解 · 英式中文珠子 → floor 英文**

| [HOOK · rel-who] | 我经常跟我儿子 Muye 一起读它，而且里面的主角动物（在里面的） 是 一只小毛毛虫。 | I read it a lot with my son Muye, and the main animal in it is a little caterpillar. |
| [PLOT] | 所以它基本上 是 讲这只又饿又小的毛毛虫。 | So it's basically about this hungry little caterpillar. |
| [PLOT] | 它一开始很小，然后它一路吃穿各种各样的食物，变得越来越大。 | It starts off small, then it eats its way through all kinds of food and gets bigger and bigger. |
| [PLOT · so-tag] | 而酷的那部分 是 它变成一只漂亮的蝴蝶，所以孩子们真的很爱那一小段。 | And the cool part is it turns into a beautiful butterfly, so the kids really love that bit. |
| [WISH-BEAT] | 我读它主要是因为 Muye 几乎每晚都要，通常在他上床睡觉之前。 | I read it mainly because Muye asks for it almost every night, usually before he goes to bed. |
| [WISH-BEAT · so-tag] | 而且老实说我也挺享受它的，所以我其实不太介意再读一遍。 | And honestly I quite enjoy it too, so I don't really mind reading it again. |

> OK so the book I'd like to talk about is a children's picture book, 'The Very Hungry Caterpillar.' I read it a lot with my son Muye, and the main animal in it is a little caterpillar. So it's basically about this hungry little caterpillar. It starts off small, then it eats its way through all kinds of food and gets bigger and bigger. And the cool part is it turns into a beautiful butterfly, so the kids really love that bit. I read it mainly because Muye asks for it almost every night, usually before he goes to bed. And honestly I quite enjoy it too, so I don't really mind reading it again. So I think it's a lovely little story, simple but the pictures are colourful and cute. It teaches kids about change and growing up, so it actually means something. So for me, even though it's a kids' book, it's really well done.

| [EXPLAIN=LAND] | 所以说实话，像那样的朋友挺少见的，而且我就觉得幸运，能有他。 | So honestly, friends like that are pretty rare, and I just feel lucky to have him. |

**②整段（连起来 shadow）**

> OK so the person I'd like to talk about is my friend Zhangwei, a developer too — basically the kind of guy everyone turns to when they're stuck. What really stands out about him is that he stays calm under pressure, and he doesn't just give you the answer — he takes the time to make you actually understand it. So a while back I was stuck on a really hard problem at work. I'd been trying to fix it for days and just couldn't work it out, so in the end I asked him for help. And I remember he just sat down with me and walked me through it, one step at a time. He showed me where I was going wrong, so it finally made sense. And the whole time he was super patient and never made me feel dumb for asking. The reason I felt so thankful is that he didn't just fix it for me — he made me understand it too. And for him, helping isn't a chore — it's a way of learning, so he's always happy to do it. So honestly, friends like that are pretty rare, and I just feel lucky to have him.

> Pretty important, I'd say. Because school is where kids learn how to think, so it shapes how they deal with problems later on. For example, a good teacher doesn't just feed you facts, but teaches you how to find answers yourself. Having said that, I think making kids kind and happy matters just as much as making them smart.

|---|---|---|
| [HOOK] | 所以我想聊的那条环保法 是 那条规则，[用来]减少塑料的（那种你只用一次的）。 | So the environmental law I'd like to talk about is the rule that cuts down on plastic you only use once. |
| [HOOK · like] | 它就是那条规则（在那里，在商店里，他们不再免费发塑料袋了）。 | It's the rule where, in shops, they don't give out free plastic bags any more. |
| [平移 · so-tag] | 现在你去购物的时候，你要么自己带袋子，要么花钱买一个，所以人们最后用的塑料少多了。 | Now when you go shopping, you either bring your own bag or you pay for one, so people end up using a lot less plastic. |
| [FACT-DROP · rel-who] | 我最早听说这个 是通过我老婆，她在城市管理部门工作的。 | I first heard about it through my wife, who works in city management. |
| [FACT-DROP · so-tag] | 她就是处理这类政策的，所以就是她告诉我这事的。 | She deals with this kind of policy, so she's the one who told me about it. |
| [CORE] | 至于谁受益，对我来说，它真的就归结到每个人，但主要是环境和我们的孩子。 | As for who benefits, for me it really comes down to everyone, but mostly the environment and our kids. |
| [CORE · like] | 那是因为更少的塑料意味着更少的垃圾，比如所有那些东西，最后跑到河里和海里的。 | That's because less plastic means less rubbish, like all the stuff that ends up in the rivers and the sea. |
| [WEIGH] | 大多数人会支持这样一条法律，虽然有些商店不会那么乐意，因为它让他们花点钱。 | Most people would back a law like this, although some shops wouldn't be as keen, because it costs them a bit. |
| [WEIGH] | 但说实话，对我来说它是件小事，一件真的有帮助的。 | But honestly, for me it's a small thing that really helps. |
| [EXPLAIN=LAND · so-tag] | 所以我觉得它是条好法律，他们只是需要让人们真的去遵守它。 | So I think it's a good law, they just need to make people actually follow it. |

**②整段（连起来 shadow）**

> So the environmental law I'd like to talk about is the rule that cuts down on plastic you only use once. It's the rule where, in shops, they don't give out free plastic bags any more. Now when you go shopping, you either bring your own bag or you pay for one, so people end up using a lot less plastic. I first heard about it through my wife, who works in city management. She deals with this kind of policy, so she's the one who told me about it. As for who benefits, for me it really comes down to everyone, but mostly the environment and our kids. That's because less plastic means less rubbish, like all the stuff that ends up in the rivers and the sea. Most people would back a law like this, although some shops wouldn't be as keen, because it costs them a bit. But honestly, for me it's a small thing that really helps. So I think it's a good law, they just need to make people actually follow it.

> OK so one time that comes to mind is when I emailed someone I went to school with. I hadn't talked to him in years, so it felt a bit awkward to reach out, out of nowhere. It turned out he'd just been really busy, so it wasn't about me at all. Honestly, it was a bit frustrating and a little embarrassing, Because the silence kind of makes you doubt yourself. So the waiting part was really the worst. So for me, it was all fine once he finally replied.

| e.g. | 比如，发条信息，对方有空了再回，所以谁都不用被打断。 | For example, with a text the other person can reply when they're free, so nobody gets interrupted. |

> Mainly for the convenience, I'd say. Because with a message you can take your time to think what to say, whereas on a call you have to react on the spot. For example, with a text the other person can reply when they're free, so nobody gets interrupted. Having said that, for something urgent I'd still call.

For example, you're waiting for an important answer but hear nothing for ages, so you start overthinking. Honestly, though, most of the time they're just busy.

| [NARRATE · to-purpose] | 所以他继续坚持，每天学一点，上网课，还做一些小项目，来练习。 | So he kept going, studied a little every day, did online classes, and built small projects to practice. |
| [NARRATE · so-tag] | 最后，大概六个月之后他就上手了，所以他拿到了那份新工作。 | In the end, after about six months he got the hang of it, so he landed the new job. |

**②整段（连起来 shadow）**

> So the person I'd like to talk about is my friend Zhangwei, who completely switched his career. What really stands out about him is that he stuck with it even when it got really hard. So the thing was, he had no background in machine learning at all. And then a few years ago his company started moving into AI, so suddenly he had to learn a whole new field from scratch, while still doing his day job. And honestly, a lot of people would've just quit. So he kept going, studied a little every day, did online classes, and built small projects to practice. In the end, after about six months he got the hang of it, so he landed the new job. The reason I really admire him is that he taught himself the whole thing from zero, because he never gave up even when it was hard. So it shows that if you keep at it, you can pretty much learn anything. So for me, he's a good reminder that hard work really pays off.

## 新26 · A time you changed an important opinion (about what success means)　(~118s)

**①拆解 · 英式中文珠子 → floor 英文**

| [BESPOKE-before] | 我年轻的时候，我以前觉得 成功 全都是关于 进一所很好的学校，然后一份工作，在一家有名的公司，所以基本上，它看起来对别人越好，就越好。 | When I was younger I used to think success was all about getting into a really good school and then a job at a famous company, so basically, the better it looked to other people, the better. |
| [BESPOKE-after] | 但后来我看到一些人（那些在纸面上看起来成功、但其实并不快乐的），还有另一些人（有着很简单的生活、看起来很快乐的），所以现在我觉得 快乐 和 做你喜欢的事（这件事）重要得多，比起只是看起来成功。 | But then I saw people who looked successful on paper but weren't actually happy, and others with really simple lives who seemed really happy, so now I think being happy and doing what you like matters way more than just looking successful. |
| [CORE] | 对我来说，它真正归结为 感觉放松，而不是 总在追下一个东西。 | For me it really comes down to feeling relaxed instead of always chasing the next thing. |

**②整段（连起来 shadow）**

> OK so one time that comes to mind is when I changed my mind about something big — what it really means to be successful — and it was after I'd been working for a few years. When I was younger I used to think success was all about getting into a really good school and then a job at a famous company, so basically, the better it looked to other people, the better. But then I saw people who looked successful on paper but weren't actually happy, and others with really simple lives who seemed really happy, so now I think being happy and doing what you like matters way more than just looking successful. For me it really comes down to feeling relaxed instead of always chasing the next thing. And honestly, changing my mind took a lot of pressure off, because I stopped comparing myself to everyone else, so I just felt way less worried.
