| [WEIGH] | 但说实话，对我来说它还是值得的，因为每个人都想要更干净的空气。 | But honestly, for me it's still worth it, because everyone wants cleaner air. |
| [CORE] | 还有我对它的感觉——对我来说它真的归结为这一点：我们不能一直把经济放在人们健康的前面。 | And how I feel about it — for me it really comes down to this: we can't keep putting the economy ahead of people's health. |
| [CORE] | 所以一条法律，像那样的，我觉得它是值得的。 | So a law like that, I think it's worth it. |

**②整段（连起来 shadow）**

> So if I could introduce one law, it'd be one that puts much stricter limits on factory pollution. It'd basically force factories to control what they release into the air and water, which is way stricter than the rules we have now. The reason people should follow it is it's really about protecting everyone's health, because if factories are allowed to pollute freely, it's ordinary people who suffer in the end. They're the ones who breathe that air, not the owners, so for me it's just the right thing to do. As for whether it'd be popular, most ordinary people would be for it, although some factory owners wouldn't, because cleaning up costs them money. But honestly, for me it's still worth it, because everyone wants cleaner air. And how I feel about it — for me it really comes down to this: we can't keep putting the economy ahead of people's health. So a law like that, I think it's worth it.

**③ P3 follow-ups**

**Q: How does technology affect the law?** → [REASON]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|

| because | 因为有(there's)到处都是的摄像头和传感器，政府就能 立刻抓到 那些违规的人(people breaking the rules)。 | because there are cameras and sensors everywhere, so the government can catch people breaking the rules straight away. |
| e.g. | 比如在成都，路上的摄像头 会自动罚款、罚 那些排放太多的车。 | For example, in Chengdu, cameras on the road automatically fine cars that pollute too much. |
| LAND | 话虽如此，科技也能帮人 绕过法律。 | Having said that, technology can also help people get around the law. |

> Mainly it makes enforcing the law easier, I'd say. Because there are cameras and sensors everywhere, so the government can catch people breaking the rules straight away. For example, in Chengdu, cameras on the road automatically fine cars that pollute too much. Having said that, technology can also help people get around the law.

> Quite a few, actually. The biggest one is probably about uniforms — everyone has to wear the same clothes. There are rules about phones too — most schools don't let you bring one in. To be fair, some of them are quite strict.

| e.g. | 比如，有些国家禁止的东西，别的国家 觉得完全正常。 | For example, some countries ban things that other countries see as totally normal. |

> I doubt it, honestly. Because different countries have very different cultures, so what feels fair in one place feels wrong in another. For example, some countries ban things that other countries see as totally normal. So I'd say a few basic ones might work, but not all of them.

| e.g. | 还有 关于工厂的规定；它们不能 就那样把废料 倒进河里。 | There are also rules about factories — they can't just dump their waste into rivers. |
| LAND | 说句公道话，不是每个人 都好好遵守。 | To be fair, not everyone follows them properly. |

> Loads, actually. The biggest one is probably rubbish sorting — you have to separate your waste into different bins. There are also rules about factories — they can't just dump their waste into rivers. To be fair, not everyone follows them properly.

## 老01 · A perfect job you'd like to have in the future (freedom-over-money tech work)　(~118s)

**①拆解 · 英式中文珠子 → floor 英文**

| 拍/模式 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| [HOOK] | 所以一份工作（我很想要的）是这样一种东西，[在里面]我能去做有趣的技术难题，但有着很多自由，来支配我自己的时间。 | So a job I'd love to have is something where I get to work on interesting tech problems, but with a lot of freedom over my own time. |
| [平移 · the-kind-of] | 它会是那种工作，[在那里]没人告诉我该做什么，有点像做我自己的老板。 | It'd be the kind of job where nobody's telling me what to do, kind of like being my own boss. |
| [NARRATE-lite] | 我慢慢想明白了这一点，这些年下来，在做了一阵子普通办公室工作之后，然后我意识到，我真正在乎的东西不是钱、也不是职位头衔。 | I slowly figured this out over the years, after doing a normal office job for a while, and I realised what I really care about isn't the money or the job title. |
| [WISH-BEAT] | 为了到那一步，我得不断把我的技术能力练得更好，并且学会逼自己一把，在没有老板的时候，这样我就能真的支配我自己的时间。 | To get there I'd need to keep getting better at my tech skills, and learn to push myself when there's no boss, so I could actually run my own time. |
| [CORE] | 而那个原因（它对我会很完美的）真的归结到自由。 | And the reason it'd be perfect for me really comes down to freedom. |
| [EXPLAIN=LAND · so-tag] | 它让我做我喜欢的事，并且还能有一个不错的工作生活平衡，所以对我来说，那件事重要得多，比一大笔薪水，因为自由说实话就是那个东西（我最在乎的）。 | It lets me do what I enjoy and still have a good work-life balance, so for me that matters way more than a big paycheck, because freedom is honestly the thing I care about most. |

**②整段（连起来 shadow）**

> So a job I'd love to have is something where I get to work on interesting tech problems, but with a lot of freedom over my own time. It'd be the kind of job where nobody's telling me what to do, kind of like being my own boss. I slowly figured this out over the years, after doing a normal office job for a while, and I realised what I really care about isn't the money or the job title. To get there I'd need to keep getting better at my tech skills, and learn to push myself when there's no boss, so I could actually run my own time. And the reason it'd be perfect for me really comes down to freedom. It lets me do what I enjoy and still have a good work-life balance, so for me that matters way more than a big paycheck, because freedom is honestly the thing I care about most.

## 老02 · Famous person you'd like to meet — Christopher Nolan　(~118s)

**①拆解 · 英式中文珠子 → floor 英文**

| 拍/模式 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| [HOOK] | 所以 我最想见的那个人 是 Christopher Nolan，那个电影导演，而且我老实说 是个超级粉丝。 | So the person I would most like to meet is Christopher Nolan, the film director, and I am honestly a huge fan. |
| [NARRATE-lite] | 我一开始认识他 是通过他的电影，比如《盗梦空间》和《星际穿越》，而且我基本上 全都看过了。 | I first got to know him through his movies, like Inception and Interstellar, and I have watched pretty much all of them. |
| [WISH-BEAT] | 我很想见到他，在某个轻松又安静的地方，比如一场访谈，这样我们能真的聊聊，而不是只拍张照。 | I would love to meet him somewhere chill and quiet, like an interview, so we could actually talk instead of just taking a photo. |
| [CORE] | 他身上真正突出的那一点 是 他的电影 就是特别有创意。 | What really stands out about him is that his movies are just so creative. |
| [EXPLAIN=LAND] | 我想见他的原因 是 我一直好奇 他是怎么想出这些的，所以我很想问问 他的点子是从哪来的。 | The reason I would want to meet him is that I always wonder how he comes up with them, so I would love to ask where his ideas come from. |
| [EXPLAIN=LAND] | 而且我想问问 他是怎么让这么复杂的想法 变得那么好懂的，因为对我来说 他会是 那个我最想见的人。 | And I would want to ask how he makes such complicated ideas easy to follow, because for me he would be the one person I would most love to meet. |

**②整段（连起来 shadow）**

> So the person I would most like to meet is Christopher Nolan, the film director, and I am honestly a huge fan. I first got to know him through his movies, like Inception and Interstellar, and I have watched pretty much all of them. I would love to meet him somewhere chill and quiet, like an interview, so we could actually talk instead of just taking a photo. What really stands out about him is that his movies are just so creative. The reason I would want to meet him is that I always wonder how he comes up with them, so I would love to ask where his ideas come from. And I would want to ask how he makes such complicated ideas easy to follow, because for me he would be the one person I would most love to meet.

## 老03 · An occasion when you were not allowed to use your mobile phone (an important work meeting)　(~115s)

**①拆解 · 英式中文珠子 → floor 英文**

| 拍/模式 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| [HOOK · dash-unpack] | 所以 一次（想到的那个）是 当我不能用我的手机的时候，在一个重要会议期间、在公司——我们得上交我们的手机，在进去之前。 | So one time that comes to mind is when I couldn't use my phone during an important meeting at work — we had to hand in our phones before going in. |
| [点名] | 它就在一个会议室里，在办公室，一个完全普通的工作会议，没什么特别的。 | It was just in a meeting room at the office, a totally normal work meeting, nothing fancy. |
| [FACT-DROP] | 原因 一部分是 为了让大家专注，一部分是 因为有些东西是私密的。 | The reason was partly to keep people focused, and partly because some of the stuff was private. |
| [NARRATE] | 一开始 它感觉真的很奇怪，没有它，因为我太习惯查看我的手机了。 | At first it felt really weird without it, because I'm so used to checking my phone. |
| [NARRATE] | 但过了一会儿 它其实还挺不错的——每个人都专注多了，而且会议进行得快多了。 | But after a while it was actually kind of nice — everyone was way more focused, and the meeting went a lot faster. |
| [NARRATE · so-tag] | 所以最后 我觉得它是件好事，因为它让我看到 我查看我的手机，当我根本不需要的时候。 | So in the end I felt it was a good thing, because it showed me I check my phone when I don't even need to. |
| [CORE] | 对我来说 它真的归结于 意识到 我有多依赖它，没有真正的理由。 | For me it really comes down to realising how much I rely on it for no real reason. |

**②整段（连起来 shadow）**

> So one time that comes to mind is when I couldn't use my phone during an important meeting at work — we had to hand in our phones before going in. It was just in a meeting room at the office, a totally normal work meeting, nothing fancy. The reason was partly to keep people focused, and partly because some of the stuff was private. At first it felt really weird without it, because I'm so used to checking my phone. But after a while it was actually kind of nice — everyone was way more focused, and the meeting went a lot faster. So in the end I felt it was a good thing, because it showed me I check my phone when I don't even need to. For me it really comes down to realising how much I rely on it for no real reason.

**③ P3 follow-ups**

**Q: How do young and old people use mobile phones differently?** → [COMPARE]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| STANCE | 我觉得，他们用得挺不一样的。 | They're quite different, I reckon. |
| 咬差异 | 年轻人 用它 干所有事——刷 社媒、玩 游戏，而老人 大多 只 打电话、发消息。 | Young people use it for everything — social media and games, whereas old people mostly just make calls and send messages. |
| LAND | 所以说到底，重点就是 他们怎么看这东西——年轻人 把它当 整个生活，老人 只当 一个打电话的工具。 | So it's really about how they see it — young people treat it as their whole life, old people just as a way to make calls. |

> They're quite different, I reckon. Young people use it for everything — social media and games, whereas old people mostly just make calls and send messages. So it's really about how they see it — young people treat it as their whole life, old people just as a way to make calls.

**Q: What positive and negative impact do mobile phones have on friendship?** → [TWO-SIDED]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| STANCE | 说实话，好坏参半。 | It's a bit of a mixed bag, honestly. |
| plus | 最大的好处是 你能 跟 那些 离得远的 朋友 保持联系，随时。 | The big plus is you can stay in touch with friends who live far away, anytime. |
| e.g. | 比如，我 每天 都 跟 我的老同学 发消息，尽管 我们 在 不同的城市。 | For example, I message my old classmates every day, even though we're in different cities. |
| LAND | 话虽如此，有时候 人们 就算 坐在一起、也 一直 盯着 手机，所以 就 不怎么 真的 聊天了。 | Having said that, sometimes people just stare at their phones even when they're sitting together, so they don't really talk. |

> It's a bit of a mixed bag, honestly. The big plus is you can stay in touch with friends who live far away, anytime. For example, I message my old classmates every day, even though we're in different cities. Having said that, sometimes people just stare at their phones even when they're sitting together, so they don't really talk.

**Q: Is it a waste of time to take pictures with mobile phones?** → [OPINION]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| STANCE | 不算 浪费吧，不。 | Not really, no. |
| because | 因为 照片 帮 你 记住 那些 美好的时刻，所以 以后 你 能 回头看。 | because photos help you remember the good moments, so later you can look back. |
| e.g. | 比如，我 旅行的时候 会 拍照，几年后 它们 能 带回 整趟 旅程。 | For instance, I take photos when I travel, and years later they bring back the whole trip. |
| LAND | 话虽如此，如果 你 花 全部时间 拍照、忘了 去 享受，那 就 有点 过了。 | Having said that, if you spend all your time taking photos and forget to enjoy it, then it's a bit much. |

> Not really, no. Because photos help you remember the good moments, so later you can look back. For instance, I take photos when I travel, and years later they bring back the whole trip. Having said that, if you spend all your time taking photos and forget to enjoy it, then it's a bit much.

**Q: Do you think it is necessary to have laws on the use of mobile phones?** → [OPINION]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| STANCE | 绝对需要。 | Definitely. |
| because | 因为 开车的时候 玩手机 真的 很危险，所以 我们 需要 规定 [去] 阻止它。 | because using your phone while driving is really dangerous, so we need rules to stop it. |
| e.g. | 比如，很多 事故 发生 就是因为(because) 人们 边 发消息 边 开车。 | For example, a lot of accidents happen because people text and drive. |
| LAND | 话虽如此，我们 没法 管 人们 在手机上 做的 每一件事。 | Having said that, we can't control everything people do on their phones. |

> Definitely. Because using your phone while driving is really dangerous, so we need rules to stop it. For example, a lot of accidents happen because people text and drive. Having said that, we can't control everything people do on their phones.

**Q: What are examples of good and poor phone manners?** → [ENUMERATE]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|

| e.g.(好) | 比如，跟 别人 吃饭的时候 把 手机 收起来。 | Like putting your phone away when you're eating with someone. |

> The biggest one's putting your phone on silent, I'd say. Like putting your phone away when you're eating with someone. A poor one is answering calls loudly on a bus, which really annoys people.

| e.g. | 比如，如果 我 有 什么 不懂的，我 就 一搜，几秒钟 就 得到 答案。 | For instance, if there's something I don't know, I just search it and get the answer in seconds. |
| LAND | 话虽如此，它 也 让 人们 更容易 保持联系，不管 离多远。 | Having said that, it also makes it easier for people to stay in touch, no matter how far apart. |

> Loads of ways, actually. The biggest one's information — you can find anything instantly. For instance, if there's something I don't know, I just search it and get the answer in seconds. Having said that, it also makes it easier for people to stay in touch, no matter how far apart.

## 老04 · A time you gave advice to others　(~122s)

**①拆解 · 英式中文珠子 → floor 英文**

| 拍/模式 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| [HOOK · rel-who] | 好，那 一次我想到的 就是 那次 我给了一些职业建议、给我一个年轻的同事，（那个才刚开始工作没多久的）。 | OK so one time that comes to mind is when I gave some career advice to a younger colleague of mine, who'd only just started working. |
| [NARRATE · so-tag] | 他在想着 跳去另一家公司，就因为 那边的工资 高了一点，所以他来找我、问我是怎么想的。 | He was thinking about jumping to another company, just because the pay there was a bit higher, so he came to me to ask what I thought. |
| [NARRATE] | 所以我跟他说，你看，在你职业的早期，别只追着钱跑——反而 要看重 你能学到多少、能成长多少。 | So I told him, look, early in your career, don't just chase the money — focus on how much you can learn and grow instead. |
| [NARRATE · so-tag] | 我说，那些技能（你现在攒下的），从长远看会回报大得多，所以那件事 比一点点涨薪 更重要。 | I said the skills you build now will pay off much more in the long run, so that matters more than a small pay bump. |
| [NARRATE] | 然后到最后，他决定留下来，而且他后来还为这事谢了我。 | And in the end he decided to stay, and he thanked me for it later. |
| [EXPLAIN=LAND] | 我给他那个建议的原因 是 我自己也犯过同样的错，在我更年轻的时候，所以我不想让他走上同一条路。 | The reason I gave him that advice is that I made the same mistake myself when I was younger, so I didn't want him to go down the same road. |
| [EXPLAIN=LAND] | 所以说实话，那感觉真的挺好的，就是知道 我自己的经验 真的能帮到某个人。 | So honestly it felt really good to know that my own experience could actually help someone out. |

**②整段（连起来 shadow）**

> OK so one time that comes to mind is when I gave some career advice to a younger colleague of mine, who'd only just started working. He was thinking about jumping to another company, just because the pay there was a bit higher, so he came to me to ask what I thought. So I told him, look, early in your career, don't just chase the money — focus on how much you can learn and grow instead. I said the skills you build now will pay off much more in the long run, so that matters more than a small pay bump. And in the end he decided to stay, and he thanked me for it later. The reason I gave him that advice is that I made the same mistake myself when I was younger, so I didn't want him to go down the same road. So honestly it felt really good to know that my own experience could actually help someone out.

**③ P3 follow-ups**

**Q: Should people prepare before giving advice?** → [OPINION]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| STANCE | 绝对的。 | Absolutely. |
| because | 我会说，主要是因为(because)如果你没先搞懂(这件事)他们的处境，你可能给出建议、(那些)根本不搭调的。 | I'd say it's mainly because if you don't understand their situation first, you might give advice that doesn't really fit. |
| e.g. | 比如，(这件事)先问几个问题、往往会让(这件事)你说的东西 有用得多。 | For example, asking a few questions first usually makes what you say much more useful. |
| LAND | 话说回来，如果只是朋友间随口聊聊，你也不用想太多。 | Having said that, if it's just a casual chat between friends, you don't need to overthink it. |

> Absolutely. I'd say it's mainly because if you don't understand their situation first, you might give advice that doesn't really fit. For example, asking a few questions first usually makes what you say much more useful. Having said that, if it's just a casual chat between friends, you don't need to overthink it.

**Q: Is it good to ask advice from strangers online?** → [OPINION]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| STANCE | 老实说，(这件事)好不好取决于(depends on)什么话题。 | Honestly, it depends on the topic. |
| because | 对于普通的事，比如(这件事)修一个东西，陌生人常常真的挺有帮助的，因为你能得到很多不同的看法。 | For everyday stuff, like fixing something, strangers can actually be really helpful, because you get lots of different views. |
| e.g. | 但对于私人的事，我不会那么做，因为你不知道他们是谁。 | But for personal things, I wouldn't do it, because you don't know who they are. |
| LAND | 所以我会说，(这件事)真的看你在问的是什么。 | So I'd say it really depends on what you're asking about. |

> Honestly, it depends on the topic. For everyday stuff, like fixing something, strangers can actually be really helpful, because you get lots of different views. But for personal things, I wouldn't do it, because you don't know who they are. So I'd say it really depends on what you're asking about.

**Q: What are the personalities of people whose job is to give advice to others?** → [ENUMERATE]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|

| because | 因为你得听人家把话讲完，才能真正搞懂(这件事)他们要什么。 | Because you have to let people finish before you really get what they want. |
| e.g. | 比如一个好的顾问，他们不会评判你，就只是听。 | Like a good counsellor, they don't judge you, they just listen. |
| LAND | 话说回来，有点自信也是要的，不然没人会信你。 | Having said that, a bit of confidence helps too, or no one will trust you. |

> The biggest one's patience, I'd say. Because you have to let people finish before you really get what they want. Like a good counsellor, they don't judge you, they just listen. Having said that, a bit of confidence helps too, or no one will trust you.

**Q: What are the problems if you ask too many people for advice?** → [ENUMERATE]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| STANCE | 我会说，最大的问题就是你会被搞糊涂。 | The biggest problem, I'd say, is you just get confused. |
| because | 因为每个人告诉你不同的东西，到最后你不知道(这件事)该信谁。 | Because everyone tells you something different, and in the end you don't know who to trust. |
| e.g. | 比如(这件事)问十个人 你该不该换工作，你会得到十个不同的答案。 | For example, asking ten people whether you should change jobs, you'll get ten different answers. |
| LAND | 话说回来，多听一两个人 通常还是好的。 | Having said that, hearing one or two extra people is usually still a good thing. |

> The biggest problem, I'd say, is you just get confused. Because everyone tells you something different, and in the end you don't know who to trust. For example, asking ten people whether you should change jobs, you'll get ten different answers. Having said that, hearing one or two extra people is usually still a good thing.

**Q: Why do some people think it is better to ask for advice from friends than from parents?** → [REASON]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|

| because | 因为父母是在一个不同的年代长大的，所以他们的建议 有时候感觉有点过时了。 | Because parents grew up in a different time, so their advice can feel a bit out of date. |
| e.g. | 比如聊感情的事，大部分人 更愿意找朋友。 | Like with relationship stuff, most people would rather go to a friend. |
| LAND | 话说回来，父母的建议 通常有更多的人生经验在里面。 | Having said that, parents' advice usually has more life experience behind it. |

> Mainly because friends understand what you're going through, I'd say. Because parents grew up in a different time, so their advice can feel a bit out of date. Like with relationship stuff, most people would rather go to a friend. Having said that, parents' advice usually has more life experience behind it.

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |

| because | 因为现在什么都在手机上，(这件事)年轻人长在里面，所以他们就是更懂。 | Because everything's on the phone now, and young people grew up with it, so they just get it better. |
| e.g. | 比如我爸妈会问我(这件事)怎么在网上付钱、或者怎么用一个新的 app。 | For example, my parents ask me how to pay online, or how to use a new app. |
| LAND | 话说回来，遇到大的人生决定，他们还是靠自己。 | Having said that, for big life decisions, they still rely on themselves. |

> Mostly with tech stuff, I'd say. Because everything's on the phone now, and young people grew up with it, so they just get it better. For example, my parents ask me how to pay online, or how to use a new app. Having said that, for big life decisions, they still rely on themselves.

## 老05 · A piece of technology (not a phone) you'd like to own — VR headset　(~120s)

**①拆解 · 英式中文珠子 → floor 英文**

| 拍/模式 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| [HOOK] | 所以我很想拥有的一个科技产品 是 一个好的 VR 头显。 | So a piece of tech I'd really love to own is a good VR headset. |
| [CORE · the-kind-of] | 我爱它的那一点 是 它感觉就像你在一个完全不同的世界里 —— 它不是一个手机，它是那种东西（你戴在头上的）。 | The thing I love about it is it feels like you're in a whole other world — it's not a phone, it's the kind of thing you wear on your head. |
| [FACT-DROP] | 那些好的 大概要花几千块钱，所以我其实还没有一个。 | The good ones cost around a few thousand yuan, so I don't actually have one yet. |
| [NARRATE-lite] | 我最开始了解到它 是通过一些视频（网上的），而且我的一个朋友有一个，所以我试过它几次 —— 而在我戴上它的那一刻，我就上瘾了。 | I first got to know about it through some videos online, and a friend of mine has one, so I've tried it a couple of times — and the moment I put it on, I was hooked. |
| [WISH-BEAT] | 我很想拥有一个，这样我就能玩游戏（那些感觉很真实的），并且去探索地方（那些甚至都不真实的）。 | I'd love to own one so I could play games that feel real, and explore places that aren't even real. |
| [EXPLAIN=LAND] | 我想要一个的原因 是 我一直都很喜欢科幻，因为一个 VR 头显 是你能达到的最接近的东西，去真正走进那些世界，而不是只是在一个屏幕上看它们。 | The reason I'd love one is I've always been really into sci-fi, because a VR headset is the closest you get to actually stepping inside those worlds instead of just watching them on a screen. |
| [EXPLAIN=LAND] | 所以说实话，它会感觉就像活在一部科幻电影里。 | So it'd honestly feel like living in a sci-fi film. |
| [EXPLAIN=LAND] | 所以对我来说，它绝对在我的愿望清单上。 | So for me, it's definitely on my wish list. |

**②整段（连起来 shadow）**

> So a piece of tech I'd really love to own is a good VR headset. The thing I love about it is it feels like you're in a whole other world — it's not a phone, it's the kind of thing you wear on your head. The good ones cost around a few thousand yuan, so I don't actually have one yet. I first got to know about it through some videos online, and a friend of mine has one, so I've tried it a couple of times — and the moment I put it on, I was hooked. I'd love to own one so I could play games that feel real, and explore places that aren't even real. The reason I'd love one is I've always been really into sci-fi, because a VR headset is the closest you get to actually stepping inside those worlds instead of just watching them on a screen. So it'd honestly feel like living in a sci-fi film. So for me, it's definitely on my wish list.

|---|---|---|
| [HOOK · rel-who] | 所以我想聊的那个人 是 我的朋友张伟，(他)是个开发者、我认识的。 | So the person I'd like to talk about is my friend Zhangwei, who's a developer I know. |
| [CORE] | 他身上真正突出的那点 是 他很擅长做计划。 | What really stands out about him is he's really good at planning. |
| [NARRATE-lite] | 我们从大学起就认识彼此，所以那就是我怎么认识他的。 | We've known each other since university, so that's how I got to know him. |
| [习惯] | 他通常做的事 是，他把他的目标规划出来、在一个叫Notion的app里，他回顾他这周过得怎么样、每个星期天，而且他基本上把他整个人生 像一个工作项目一样来运营。 | What he usually does is, he maps out his goals in an app called Notion, he looks back at how his week went every Sunday, and he basically runs his whole life like a work project. |
| [习惯] | 他把它规划出来、按年，每几个月，甚至每一个月。 | He plans it out for the year, every few months, even every single month. |
| [EXPLAIN=LAND] | 说实话我真的挺佩服他，因为我自己算是个有点乱的人，所以 看到他对自己有多严格（这件事）让我想做同样的事。 | Honestly I really admire him, because I'm kind of a messy person myself, so seeing how strict he is with himself makes me want to do the same. |
| [EXPLAIN=LAND] | 而且事情是这样，他不只是做计划，他真的坚持执行它们，（那个）才是难的部分。 | And the thing is, he doesn't just make plans, he actually sticks to them, which is the hard part. |
| [EXPLAIN=LAND] | 所以对我来说，他是一个很好的提醒，(就是)做计划真的会有回报。 | So for me, he's a good reminder that planning really does pay off. |

**②整段（连起来 shadow）**

> So the person I'd like to talk about is my friend Zhangwei, who's a developer I know. What really stands out about him is he's really good at planning. We've known each other since university, so that's how I got to know him. What he usually does is, he maps out his goals in an app called Notion, he looks back at how his week went every Sunday, and he basically runs his whole life like a work project. He plans it out for the year, every few months, even every single month. Honestly I really admire him, because I'm kind of a messy person myself, so seeing how strict he is with himself makes me want to do the same. And the thing is, he doesn't just make plans, he actually sticks to them, which is the hard part. So for me, he's a good reminder that planning really does pay off.

**③ P3 follow-ups**

**Q: Do you think it's important to plan ahead?** → [OPINION]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| STANCE | 绝对是。 | Definitely, yeah. |
| because | 因为(这件事)提前做计划 让你 知道 接下来会发生什么，所以你就不用 在最后一刻 手忙脚乱。 | because planning ahead lets you know what's coming, so you don't have to rush at the last minute. |
| e.g. | 比如考试，如果你 提前几周 排好复习计划，你会 感觉冷静得多。 | For example, with exams, if you plan your revision a few weeks ahead, you feel a lot calmer. |
| LAND | 话虽如此，我不会 把每件小事都 计划到死，你也得 留点灵活。 | Having said that, I wouldn't plan every little thing to death — you need to leave some flexibility. |

> Definitely, yeah. Because planning ahead lets you know what's coming, so you don't have to rush at the last minute. For example, with exams, if you plan your revision a few weeks ahead, you feel a lot calmer. Having said that, I wouldn't plan every little thing to death — you need to leave some flexibility.

**Q: What activities do we need to plan ahead?** → [ENUMERATE]

| e.g. | 还有像 婚礼 或者 大型活动，那些也 需要 好几个月的准备。 | And things like weddings or big events also need months of preparation. |

> Quite a few, actually — travel's the biggest one, I'd say. Because if you don't book flights and hotels ahead, prices go up and you might not get anything. And things like weddings or big events also need months of preparation.

**Q: Do you think children should plan their future careers?** → [OPINION]

| because | 因为 小孩子 还太小，不知道 自己想要什么，所以 把一条路 定死 太早了。 | because young kids are too small to know what they want, so locking into one path is too early. |
| e.g. | 但如果是 青少年，有个 大致方向 是好事，这样他们 知道该往哪儿努力。 | But for teenagers, having a rough direction is good, so they know what to work towards. |
| LAND | 所以我会说，方向 可以，但别 定死。 | So I'd say a direction's fine, but don't set it in stone. |

> Honestly, it depends on how old they are. Because young kids are too small to know what they want, so locking into one path is too early. But for teenagers, having a rough direction is good, so they know what to work towards. So I'd say a direction's fine, but don't set it in stone.

| e.g. | 比如 选学校，父母 可能知道 哪个 更适合，或者 哪个 花钱不值。 | For example, with choosing a school, parents might know which one fits better, or which one isn't worth the money. |
| LAND | 话虽如此，最后的 决定 还是应该 让孩子自己拿，不然 他们学不会。 | Having said that, the final decision should still be the kid's, otherwise they won't learn. |

> Absolutely. Because adults have been through it, so they can see problems that kids can't. For example, with choosing a school, parents might know which one fits better, or which one isn't worth the money. Having said that, the final decision should still be the kid's, otherwise they won't learn.

## 老07 · A child who loves drawing/painting — Muye　(~120s)

**①拆解 · 英式中文珠子 → floor 英文**

| 拍/模式 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| [HOOK] | 我想聊的那个孩子 是 我儿子，木野。 | So the child I'd like to talk about is my son, Muye. |
| [CORE] | 他五岁，而他身上真正突出的那点 是 他绝对超爱画画。 | He's five years old, and what really stands out about him is he absolutely loves drawing. |
| [NARRATE-lite · dash-unpack] | 因为他是我亲儿子，我从他很小起就一直看他画画——而他最爱画的东西 是 恐龙，所以我们家基本上被恐龙画盖满了。 | Since he's my own son, I've watched him draw ever since he was little — and his favourite thing to draw is dinosaurs, so our house is basically covered in dinosaur pictures. |
| [FACT-DROP] | 他几乎每天都画，基本上任何时候，只要他有点空闲时间。 | He draws almost every day, pretty much any time he's got some free time. |
| [习惯] | 他通常做的 是，他拿起他的笔，坐到地板上，然后就直接开始画。 | What he usually does is, he picks up his pens, sits down on the floor, and just starts drawing. |
| [EXPLAIN=LAND] | 我觉得他爱它 是因为 这是他表达自己的方式，而他对说话还有点害羞，所以画画算是他的一种方式，[去]展示他脑子里有什么。 | I think he loves it because it's how he expresses himself, and he's still a bit shy with words, so drawing is kind of his way of showing what's in his head. |
| [EXPLAIN=LAND] | 而在他画完一张之后，他总是让我们把名字写在上面，所以对我来说，它就是让他真的很开心，而且看着很温馨。 | And after he finishes one, he always asks us to write our names on it, so for me it just makes him really happy, and it's lovely to watch. |

**②整段（连起来 shadow）**

> So the child I'd like to talk about is my son, Muye. He's five years old, and what really stands out about him is he absolutely loves drawing. Since he's my own son, I've watched him draw ever since he was little — and his favourite thing to draw is dinosaurs, so our house is basically covered in dinosaur pictures. He draws almost every day, pretty much any time he's got some free time. What he usually does is, he picks up his pens, sits down on the floor, and just starts drawing. I think he loves it because it's how he expresses himself, and he's still a bit shy with words, so drawing is kind of his way of showing what's in his head. And after he finishes one, he always asks us to write our names on it, so for me it just makes him really happy, and it's lovely to watch.

**③ P3 follow-ups**

**Q: What is the right age for a child to learn drawing?** → [OPINION]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| STANCE | 说实话，越早越好。 | Honestly, the earlier the better. |
| because | 因为对小孩子来说，在他们学会担心"画得像不像"之前，(这件事)就只是玩儿。 | Because for little kids, before they learn to worry about whether it looks right, it's just play. |
| e.g. | 我是说，一个三岁的小孩，(那个)拿蜡笔乱涂的，其实也在学怎么去看颜色和形状。 | I mean, a three-year-old scribbling with crayons is actually learning how to see colours and shapes. |
| LAND | 话说回来，我不会去逼他们、[去]太早正经上课。 | Having said that, I wouldn't push them into serious lessons too early. |

> Honestly, the earlier the better. Because for little kids, before they learn to worry about whether it looks right, it's just play. I mean, a three-year-old scribbling with crayons is actually learning how to see colours and shapes. Having said that, I wouldn't push them into serious lessons too early.

**Q: Why do most children draw more often than adults do?** → [REASON]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| STANCE | 我会说，主要就是因为(because)大人太在意别人怎么看了。 | Mostly it's because adults care too much about what others think. |
| because | 小孩子还不担心画得好不好，所以他们就是画着玩、[去]开心。 | Kids don't worry yet about whether it's any good, so they just draw for fun. |
| 第二点 | 还有，大人一天到晚忙工作，(这件事)几乎不给他们剩下什么时间、[去]坐下来乱画。 | Also, adults are busy with work all day, which leaves them almost no time to sit down and doodle. |
| LAND | 话说回来，有些大人确实又捡起来了、当作放松。 | Having said that, some adults do pick it back up as a way to relax. |

> Mostly it's because adults care too much about what others think. Kids don't worry yet about whether it's any good, so they just draw for fun. Also, adults are busy with work all day, which leaves them almost no time to sit down and doodle. Having said that, some adults do pick it back up as a way to relax.

**Q: Why do some people visit galleries or museums instead of viewing artworks online?** → [REASON]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| STANCE | 我会说，主要是因为(because)亲眼看，感觉完全不一样。 | Mainly, I'd say, because seeing it in person just feels completely different. |
| because | 在屏幕上，你看不出那个真实的大小、还有那些笔触，(那些)让一幅画活起来的。 | On a screen, you can't get the real size and the brushstrokes that make a painting come alive. |
| e.g. | 我是说，站在一幅巨大的油画面前，那种震撼，一张照片根本给不了你。 | I mean, standing in front of a huge painting gives you a hit that a photo just can't. |
| LAND | 话说回来，网上看，对于随便逛逛，还是挺方便的。 | Having said that, online is still handy for a quick browse. |

> Mainly, I'd say, because seeing it in person just feels completely different. On a screen, you can't get the real size and the brushstrokes that make a painting come alive. I mean, standing in front of a huge painting gives you a hit that a photo just can't. Having said that, online is still handy for a quick browse.

**Q: Do you think galleries and museums should be free of charge?** → [OPINION]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| STANCE | 大体上应该，是的。 | Mostly, yes. |
| because | 因为艺术应该是给每个人的，而不只是给那些付得起钱的人。 | Because art should be for everyone, not just people who can afford it. |
| e.g. | 我是说，如果进去免费，那些普通家庭就更可能带孩子去了。 | I mean, if it's free to get in, ordinary families are more likely to take their kids. |
| LAND | 话说回来，特展收点小钱，我觉得挺合理。 | Having said that, charging a bit for special exhibitions seems fair enough. |

> Mostly, yes. Because art should be for everyone, not just people who can afford it. I mean, if it's free to get in, ordinary families are more likely to take their kids. Having said that, charging a bit for special exhibitions seems fair enough.

**Q: How do artworks inspire people?** → [ENUMERATE]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| STANCE | 方式可多了，其实。 | Loads of ways, actually. |
| 最大那个 | 最大的一个，我会说，是它们让你用不同的眼光去看熟悉的东西。 | The biggest one, I'd say, is they make you see familiar things in a new way. |
| e.g. | 我是说，一幅画能一下子勾起一段回忆、或者一种感觉，(那个)你都快忘了的。 | I mean, a painting can suddenly bring back a memory or a feeling you'd almost forgotten. |
| LAND | 话说回来，(这件事)真的看每个人自己。 | Having said that, it really depends on the person. |

> Loads of ways, actually. The biggest one, I'd say, is they make you see familiar things in a new way. I mean, a painting can suddenly bring back a memory or a feeling you'd almost forgotten. Having said that, it really depends on the person.

**Q: What are the differences between reading a book and visiting a museum?** → [COMPARE]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| STANCE | 我觉得，这两个挺不一样的。 | They're quite different, I reckon. |
| 咬差异 | 读书往往是安静的、一个人的事，而逛博物馆更多是往外走、[去]看真东西。 | Reading tends to be quiet and solo, whereas a museum is more about getting out and seeing the real thing. |
| e.g. | 我是说，一本书让你在脑子里去想象，可一幅画就直接摆在你眼前了。 | I mean, a book makes you picture things in your head, but a painting is right there in front of you. |
| LAND | 所以说到底，真的就是关于你想要哪一种体验。 | So it really comes down to which kind of experience you want. |

> They're quite different, I reckon. Reading tends to be quiet and solo, whereas a museum is more about getting out and seeing the real thing. I mean, a book makes you picture things in your head, but a painting is right there in front of you. So it really comes down to which kind of experience you want.

## 老08 · A program or app on your phone — Bilibili　(~120s)

**①拆解 · 英式中文珠子 → floor 英文**

| 拍/模式 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| [HOOK · appositive] | 所以我想聊的那个 app 是 Bilibili，一个视频 app（那种在中国很火的）。 | So the app I'd like to talk about is Bilibili, a video app that's really popular in China. |
| [CORE] | 我爱它的那一点 是 我能放松、同时又学到点东西。 | The thing I love about it is I can relax and learn something at the same time. |
| [FACT-DROP] | 我几乎每天都用它，通常在晚上，当我想放松的时候。 | I use it pretty much every day, usually in the evening when I want to relax. |
| [习惯] | 我通常做的是，我看科技视频、科学讲解、还有纪录片，所以我有点放空、又真的顺便学到点东西。 | What I usually do is, I watch tech videos, science explainers, and documentaries, so I kind of switch off and actually learn something too. |
| [NARRATE-lite] | 一个朋友几年前跟我说起它，然后我从那以后就一直上瘾，所以我就是这么知道它的。 | A friend told me about it years ago, and I've been hooked ever since, so that's how I heard about it. |
| [EXPLAIN=LAND] | 说实话我现在感觉我离不开它了，因为每当我想知道点什么，总有人已经做过一个视频（关于那个的）。 | Honestly I feel like I can't live without it now, because whenever I want to know something, someone's always made a video about it. |
| [EXPLAIN=LAND] | 所以我就直接打开它、搜一下。 | So I just open it and search. |
| [EXPLAIN=LAND] | 唯一的坏处是很容易忘了时间，因为一个视频就带出下一个。 | The only downside is it's easy to lose track of time, because one video just leads to another. |
| [EXPLAIN=LAND] | 但总的来说，我真的挺喜欢它的。 | But overall, I really do like it. |

**②整段（连起来 shadow）**

> So the app I'd like to talk about is Bilibili, a video app that's really popular in China. The thing I love about it is I can relax and learn something at the same time. I use it pretty much every day, usually in the evening when I want to relax. What I usually do is, I watch tech videos, science explainers, and documentaries, so I kind of switch off and actually learn something too. A friend told me about it years ago, and I've been hooked ever since, so that's how I heard about it. Honestly I feel like I can't live without it now, because whenever I want to know something, someone's always made a video about it. So I just open it and search. The only downside is it's easy to lose track of time, because one video just leads to another. But overall, I really do like it.

**③ P3 follow-ups**

**Q: What are the differences between old and young people when using apps?** → [COMPARE]

| because | 因为(because) 很多 app 收集 你的数据，所以 人们担心 它去了哪里。 | because a lot of apps collect your data, so people worry about where it goes. |
| LAND | 说句公道话，一些老年人 就是觉得 它们让人困惑。 | To be fair, some older people just find them confusing. |

| because | 因为(because) 你几乎 能在上面 做任何事——聊天、付款、订东西。 | because you can do almost everything on it — chat, pay, book stuff. |
| e.g. | 抖音 也超火，用来 看短视频。 | TikTok's huge too, for short videos. |

**Q: Should parents limit their children's use of computer programs and computer games? Why and how?** → [OPINION]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| STANCE | 绝对的。 | Definitely. |
| because | 因为(because) 小孩 没法真的 控制自己，所以 如果你让他们，他们会玩一整天。 | because kids can't really control themselves, so they'll play all day if you let them. |

> Definitely. Because kids can't really control themselves, so they'll play all day if you let them. The best way is setting a time limit, like an hour a day. Having said that, I wouldn't ban games completely — some are good for learning.

**Q: Do you think young people are more and more reliant on these programs?** → [OPINION]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| STANCE | 绝对是，对。 | Absolutely, yeah. |
| because | 因为(because) 现在 什么都在手机上，所以 他们真的 离不开它。 | because everything's on the phone now, so they can't really do without it. |

> Absolutely, yeah. Because everything's on the phone now, so they can't really do without it. Like, they use apps to study, order food, even to sleep. Having said that, it's not all bad — it saves a lot of time too.

## 老09 · An occasion when many people were smiling — Muye's birthday party　(~118s)

**①拆解 · 英式中文珠子 → floor 英文**

|---|---|---|
| [HOOK] | 一个我想到的时刻 是 那次我们给我儿子木野办生日派对，去年，在家里。 | One time that comes to mind is when we threw my son Muye's birthday party last year, at home. |
| [FACT-DROP · dash-unpack] | 我们都在那儿——我和我老婆、爷爷奶奶、还有他的几个小朋友。 | We were all there — me and my wife, the grandparents, and a few of his little friends. |
| [NARRATE] | 所以我们端出了蛋糕，我老婆给他做了一个恐龙蛋糕。 | So we brought out the cake, and my wife had made him a dinosaur cake. |
| [NARRATE] | 然后他的眼睛一下就亮了，然后大家开始唱生日快乐。 | And then his eyes just lit up, and everyone started singing happy birthday. |
| [ONE-MOMENT] | 我记得那一刻，他许了个愿，然后他就吹灭了蜡烛，带着大大的笑容。 | I remember the moment he made a wish, and he just blew out the candles with this big grin. |
| [NARRATE] | 最后，我们都鼓掌了。 | In the end, we all clapped. |
| [EXPLAIN=LAND] | 大多数人在笑的原因 是 他的兴奋就是会传染，因为当他那么开心的时候，它会传给每个人。 | The reason most people were smiling is his excitement was just contagious, because when he's that happy it spreads to everyone. |
| [EXPLAIN=LAND · so-tag] | 当你看到一个孩子那么开心，你也忍不住会笑，所以对我来说真正归结起来就是那个——它把全家人聚到一起。 | When you see a kid that happy, you can't help but smile too, so for me it really comes down to that — it brings the whole family together. |

**②整段（连起来 shadow）**

> One time that comes to mind is when we threw my son Muye's birthday party last year, at home. We were all there — me and my wife, the grandparents, and a few of his little friends. So we brought out the cake, and my wife had made him a dinosaur cake. And then his eyes just lit up, and everyone started singing happy birthday. I remember the moment he made a wish, and he just blew out the candles with this big grin. In the end, we all clapped. The reason most people were smiling is his excitement was just contagious, because when he's that happy it spreads to everyone. When you see a kid that happy, you can't help but smile too, so for me it really comes down to that — it brings the whole family together.

| LAND | 话虽如此，这(这件事) 真的因人而异。 | Having said that, it really varies from person to person. |

> I think they do, yeah. Because society kind of expects women to be friendlier and warmer, so they smile more. For example, in service jobs like reception, most are women, and they smile all day. Having said that, it really varies from person to person.

| e.g. | 比如，一个宝宝 一整天 都咯咯笑，但一个大人 需要 真正好笑的东西。 | For instance, a baby giggles all day, but a grown-up needs something genuinely funny. |

> When they're younger, I'd say. Because little kids smile at anything, whereas adults smile more when they've got a reason. For instance, a baby giggles all day, but a grown-up needs something genuinely funny. So it's really about caring more about what others think as you get older.

## 老10 · A time you felt proud of a family member — wife passed the Japanese exam　(~118s)

**①拆解 · 英式中文珠子 → floor 英文**

| [CORE] | 关于她，真正突出的那一点 是 她自己把整个东西学下来的。 | What really stands out about her is that she taught herself the whole thing. |
| [CORE] | 她已经自己学了 好几年了。 | She'd been studying on her own for a couple of years. |
| [NARRATE-lite · with-tag] | 事情是这样，她有一份全职工作，还带着一个孩子要照顾，所以她真的很忙。 | The thing is, she has a full-time job, with a kid to look after, so she's really busy. |
| [NARRATE-lite] | 但她还是每天学一点点，主要是看剧和用一个 app。 | But she still studied a little every day, mostly watching dramas and using an app. |
| [NARRATE-lite] | 然后到最后，所有那些努力 都有了回报。 | And in the end, all that effort paid off. |
| [EXPLAIN=LAND] | 我骄傲的原因 是 它需要很多自律，去自己学一门语言，因为没有人在推着她——她就是坚持下来了。 | The reason I'm proud is that it takes a lot of discipline to learn a language on your own, because no one was pushing her — she just stuck with it. |

**②整段（连起来 shadow）**

> One time that comes to mind is when I felt really proud of my wife, last year, when she passed a Japanese exam. What really stands out about her is that she taught herself the whole thing. She'd been studying on her own for a couple of years. The thing is, she has a full-time job, with a kid to look after, so she's really busy. But she still studied a little every day, mostly watching dramas and using an app. And in the end, all that effort paid off. The reason I'm proud is that it takes a lot of discipline to learn a language on your own, because no one was pushing her — she just stuck with it. So for me it shows what kind of person she is, and it makes me want to be stricter with myself too.

| e.g. | 比如，有些 博物馆 让你 戴上 眼镜、[去] 看 一座 古城 是什么样。 | For example, some museums let you put on glasses and see what an old city looked like. |
| LAND | 话虽如此，我觉得 真正的 老东西 还是 更打动人。 | Having said that, I think the real old objects still move people more. |

> The biggest one's that it makes museums more interesting, I'd say. Because now a lot of museums use screens and VR, so you can really interact with the exhibits. For example, some museums let you put on glasses and see what an old city looked like. Having said that, I think the real old objects still move people more.

**Q: What are the benefits of technology for learning history?** → [ENUMERATE]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| STANCE | 我会说，最大的好处 就是，它让 历史 感觉 更真实。 | The biggest one's that it makes history feel more real, I'd say. |
| because | 因为 比起 只是 读 书里的 文字，你 可以 看 视频 和 纪录片，所以 它 更容易 记住。 | because instead of just reading text in a book, you can watch videos and documentaries, so it's easier to remember. |
| e.g. | 比如，我 从 一部 纪录片 里 学到的 二战，比 我 从 课本 里 学到的 还多。 | For example, I learned more about World War II from one documentary than from my textbook. |
| LAND | 话虽如此，你 还是 得 小心 网上 那些 假信息。 | Having said that, you still have to be careful about fake information online. |

> The biggest one's that it makes history feel more real, I'd say. Because instead of just reading text in a book, you can watch videos and documentaries, so it's easier to remember. For example, I learned more about World War II from one documentary than from my textbook. Having said that, you still have to be careful about fake information online.

**Q: Why do people visit museums?** → [REASON]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| STANCE | 我会说，主要是 为了 学东西。 | Mainly to learn, I'd say. |
| because | 因为 博物馆 是 一个 有趣的 方式，[去] 亲眼 看到 历史，而不是 从 书里。 | because a museum is a fun way to see history with your own eyes, not from a book. |
| e.g. | 比如，很多 家庭 周末 带 孩子 去，(这件事) 让 学习 感觉 更像 玩。 | For example, a lot of families take their kids on weekends, which makes learning feel more like fun. |
| LAND | 话虽如此，有些人 去 只是 为了 打发 一个 下雨天。 | Having said that, some people go just to pass a rainy day. |

> Mainly to learn, I'd say. Because a museum is a fun way to see history with your own eyes, not from a book. For example, a lot of families take their kids on weekends, which makes learning feel more like fun. Having said that, some people go just to pass a rainy day.

## 老12 · A car trip you'd like to take — road trip to western Sichuan with family　(~118s)

**①拆解 · 英式中文珠子 → floor 英文**

| 拍/模式 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| [HOOK] | 我很想去的一趟旅行 是 一趟自驾游，跟我的家人 —— 我老婆和我儿子木也。 | A trip I'd love to take is a road trip with my family — my wife and my son Muye. |
| [WISH-BEAT] | 我很想带他们去个新地方，这样我们就能单纯一起待段时间。 | I'd love to take them somewhere new, so we could just spend some time together. |
| [WISH-BEAT] | 我很想开车去个地方，有好风景的，这样我就能慢慢来，沿着山路开，停在任何地方（看着不错的那种）。 | I'd love to drive somewhere with nice views, so I could take our time, drive along the mountain roads and stop anywhere that looks nice. |
| [点名] | 它是一块很漂亮的地方（这个国家的），就在西边的四川那儿。 | It's a really pretty part of the country, over in western Sichuan. |
| [点名] | 它出名就是因为它的山路。 | It's known for its mountain roads. |
| [FACT-DROP] | 我大概会等到木也大一点，可能过个几年，然后在暑假的时候去。 | I'd probably wait until Muye is a bit older, maybe in a few years, and go during the summer holiday. |
| [EXPLAIN=LAND · dash-unpack] | 我会开车去、而不是坐飞机的原因 是 那种自由，因为当你开车的时候，你不会被绑死在一个时间表上 —— 你可以停下来，无论什么时候你看到一个好风景，拍拍照，然后让木也到处跑跑。 | The reason I'd go by car rather than fly is the freedom, because when you drive you're not stuck to a schedule — you can stop whenever you see a nice view, take photos and let Muye run around. |
| [EXPLAIN=LAND · so-tag] | 所以旅程本身就是乐趣的一半，而一趟轻松的自驾游，跟家人一起，是我真的很期待的一件事。 | So the journey itself is half the fun, and a relaxed road trip with the family is something I'm really looking forward to. |

**②整段（连起来 shadow）**

> A trip I'd love to take is a road trip with my family — my wife and my son Muye. I'd love to take them somewhere new, so we could just spend some time together. I'd love to drive somewhere with nice views, so I could take our time, drive along the mountain roads and stop anywhere that looks nice. It's a really pretty part of the country, over in western Sichuan. It's known for its mountain roads. I'd probably wait until Muye is a bit older, maybe in a few years, and go during the summer holiday. The reason I'd go by car rather than fly is the freedom, because when you drive you're not stuck to a schedule — you can stop whenever you see a nice view, take photos and let Muye run around. So the journey itself is half the fun, and a relaxed road trip with the family is something I'm really looking forward to.

**③ P3 follow-ups**

**Q: Which form of vehicle is more popular in your country, bikes, cars or motorcycles?** → [COMPARE]

|---|---|---|

| diff | 汽车赢，是因为它们更舒适、也能带你(去)更远的地方，而摩托车 主要是给 (那些)想省钱的人。 | Cars win because they're more comfortable and can take you further, whereas motorbikes are mostly for people who want to save money. |
| e.g. | 比如，在成都，几乎每个家庭 现在都有 一辆车。 | For instance, in Chengdu, almost every family has a car now. |
| LAND | 话虽如此，电动自行车 在短途通勤上 仍然非常流行。 | Having said that, e-bikes are still really popular for short commutes. |

> Cars, definitely, these days. Cars win because they're more comfortable and can take you further, whereas motorbikes are mostly for people who want to save money. For instance, in Chengdu, almost every family has a car now. Having said that, e-bikes are still really popular for short commutes.

**Q: Do you think air pollution comes mostly from mobile vehicles?** → [OPINION]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| STANCE | 不完全是。 | Not entirely, no. |
| because | 因为汽车 确实制造 很多污染，但工厂 和 电厂(那些烧煤的) 也是 一个大来源。 | because cars do add a lot of pollution, but factories and power plants — the ones burning coal — are a big source too. |
| e.g. | 比如，在北方的冬天，供暖 让空气 糟糕得多。 | For example, in the north in winter, heating makes the air much worse. |
| LAND | 话虽如此，减少车流 肯定会有帮助。 | Having said that, cutting down on traffic would definitely help. |

> Not entirely, no. Because cars do add a lot of pollution, but factories and power plants — the ones burning coal — are a big source too. For example, in the north in winter, heating makes the air much worse. Having said that, cutting down on traffic would definitely help.

**Q: Do you think people need to change the way of transportation drastically to protect the environment?** → [OPINION]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| STANCE | 是的，某种程度上。 | Yes, to some degree. |
| because | 因为(这件事)光是买 电动车 还不够，我们还需要 更好的公交 和 地铁，(去)让人们 把车 留家里。 | because just buying electric cars isn't enough; we also need better buses and metros so people leave their cars at home. |
| e.g. | 比如，如果地铁 又快又便宜，很多人 就直接 不开车了。 | For instance, if the metro is fast and cheap, a lot of people just stop driving. |
| LAND | 话虽如此，太快改变 会给普通家庭 增加负担。 | Having said that, changing too fast would put a burden on ordinary families. |

> Yes, to some degree. Because just buying electric cars isn't enough; we also need better buses and metros so people leave their cars at home. For instance, if the metro is fast and cheap, a lot of people just stop driving. Having said that, changing too fast would put a burden on ordinary families.

**Q: How are the transportation systems in urban areas and rural areas different?** → [COMPARE]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| STANCE | 它们相当不一样，我觉得。 | They're quite different, I reckon. |
| diff | 城市 往往 有 地铁 和 公交 覆盖 每个角落，而乡下 大多靠 电动自行车 或者 走路。 | Cities tend to have metros and buses covering every corner, whereas the countryside mostly relies on e-bikes or walking. |
| e.g. | 比如，在成都 你随时能叫到 一辆车，但在我老家的村子里，你得等 很久。 | For example, in Chengdu you can get a car anytime, but in my home village you have to wait ages. |
| LAND | 所以，这真的就是 关于 有 多少人 住在那儿。 | So it's really about how many people live there. |

> They're quite different, I reckon. Cities tend to have metros and buses covering every corner, whereas the countryside mostly relies on e-bikes or walking. For example, in Chengdu you can get a car anytime, but in my home village you have to wait ages. So it's really about how many people live there.

|---|---|---|

> Mainly income, I'd say. Because more families can afford a car now, and having one gives you more freedom to go wherever you want. For example, going out with kids, a car is much more comfortable than squeezing onto a bus. Having said that, the traffic jams are much worse now too.

## 老13 · A person who solved a problem in a smart way — Zhangwei (套 新21)　(~120s)

**①拆解 · 英式中文珠子 → floor 英文**

| 拍/模式 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| [HOOK · appositive] | 我想聊的那个人 是 我朋友张伟，一个开发者（像我一样的）——基本上就是那种人，大家一卡住就会去找的。 | The person I'd like to talk about is my friend Zhangwei, a developer like me — basically the kind of guy everyone turns to when they're stuck. |
| [CORE] | 关于他真正突出的那一点 是 他真的很会解决问题，而且他在压力下保持冷静。 | What really stands out about him is he's really clever at solving problems, and he stays calm under pressure. |
| [NARRATE] | 所以前段时间 我卡在一个很难的问题上，在工作上，而且我一直在试着修它，好几天了，但我就是搞不定。 | So a while ago I was stuck on a really tough problem at work, and I'd been trying to fix it for days but I just couldn't work it out. |
| [NARRATE · so-tag] | 所以我找他帮忙，因为他就是那种人，大家都会去找的。 | So I asked him for help, because he's the kind of guy everyone turns to. |
| [NARRATE] | 而且他不只是给我答案——他坐下来和我一起，教我怎么做，一步一步地，还帮我看到我哪里做错了。 | And he didn't just give me the answer — he sat down with me and showed me how, one step at a time, and helped me see where I was going wrong. |
| [NARRATE] | 所以最后我们一起把它解决了。 | So in the end we solved it together. |
| [EXPLAIN=LAND] | 我觉得这很聪明的原因 是 他不只是打个补丁——他让我明白它为什么坏了，所以下一次我自己就能修了。 | The reason I think it was smart is he didn't just patch it — he made me understand why it broke, so the next time I could fix it myself. |
| [EXPLAIN=LAND · so-tag] | 而且对他来说，帮忙不是个苦差事——它是一种学习的方式，所以说实话，那样的朋友挺少见的。 | And for him, helping isn't a chore — it's a way of learning, so honestly, friends like that are pretty rare. |

**②整段（连起来 shadow）**

> The person I'd like to talk about is my friend Zhangwei, a developer like me — basically the kind of guy everyone turns to when they're stuck. What really stands out about him is he's really clever at solving problems, and he stays calm under pressure. So a while ago I was stuck on a really tough problem at work, and I'd been trying to fix it for days but I just couldn't work it out. So I asked him for help, because he's the kind of guy everyone turns to. And he didn't just give me the answer — he sat down with me and showed me how, one step at a time, and helped me see where I was going wrong. So in the end we solved it together. The reason I think it was smart is he didn't just patch it — he made me understand why it broke, so the next time I could fix it myself. And for him, helping isn't a chore — it's a way of learning, so honestly, friends like that are pretty rare.

**③ P3 follow-ups**

**Q: Do you think children are born smart or they learn to become smart?** → [OPINION]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| STANCE | 老实说，两者都有一点。 | Honestly, it's a bit of both. |
| because | 因为有些孩子生下来 学东西就快，但我会说，是努力 塑造 他们最终 有多强。 | because some kids are born picking things up fast, but I'd say it's the hard work that shapes how good they end up. |
| e.g. | 比如，一个有天分的孩子，如果从不练习，还是会落后于 (那些)努力的孩子。 | For example, a gifted kid who never practises will still fall behind the ones who work hard. |
| LAND | 话虽如此，我觉得起点确实 给你一点点先机。 | Having said that, I do think the starting point gives you a bit of a head start. |

> Honestly, it's a bit of both. Because some kids are born picking things up fast, but I'd say it's the hard work that shapes how good they end up. For example, a gifted kid who never practises will still fall behind the ones who work hard. Having said that, I do think the starting point gives you a bit of a head start.

**Q: How do children become smart at school?** → [ENUMERATE]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| STANCE | 其实有很多方式。 | Loads of ways, actually. |
| e.g. | 但我会说，最大的一个是 提问题、[去]搞懂 事情为什么是这样，而不是 只死记硬背。 | But I'd say the biggest one is asking questions and working out why things are the way they are, instead of just memorising. |
| because | 因为当你真正理解 一个东西背后的道理，它就 记得住。 | because when you really understand the reason behind something, it sticks. |
| LAND | 话虽如此，好老师也帮 很大忙。 | Having said that, a good teacher helps a lot too. |

> Loads of ways, actually. But I'd say the biggest one is asking questions and working out why things are the way they are, instead of just memorising. Because when you really understand the reason behind something, it sticks. Having said that, a good teacher helps a lot too.

**Q: Why are some people well-rounded and others only good at one thing?** → [REASON]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| STANCE | 我会说，主要是因为(because of)他们 怎么长大的。 | Mainly because of how they grew up, I'd say. |
| because | 因为有些人从小 就被鼓励 去尝试很多东西，而有些人 早早就选定 一个方向、[去]深挖。 | because some people are pushed to try lots of things when they're young, while others pick one direction early and go deep. |
| e.g. | 比如，一个孩子 如果什么兴趣班都上，最后就 什么都懂一点。 | For example, a kid who does every kind of class ends up knowing a bit about everything. |
| LAND | 话虽如此，两种人 社会都需要。 | Having said that, society needs both kinds. |

> Mainly because of how they grew up, I'd say. Because some people are pushed to try lots of things when they're young, while others pick one direction early and go deep. For example, a kid who does every kind of class ends up knowing a bit about everything. Having said that, society needs both kinds.

**Q: Why does modern society need talents of all kinds?** → [REASON]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| STANCE | 大概是因为(because)现在社会 太复杂了。 | Mostly it's because society is so complex now. |
| because | 因为一个国家 运转起来 需要各种各样的人——医生、工程师、也需要艺术家——你没法 只靠一种人 撑起来。 | because a country needs all sorts of people to run — doctors, engineers, and artists too — you can't run it on just one type. |
| e.g. | 比如，就算你有最好的技术，你还是需要 会讲故事的人 [去]把它卖出去。 | For example, even with the best technology, you still need people who can tell a story to sell it. |
| LAND | 话虽如此，我觉得有些技能 确实更抢手。 | Having said that, I do think some skills are more in demand than others. |

> Mostly it's because society is so complex now. Because a country needs all sorts of people to run — doctors, engineers, and artists too — you can't run it on just one type. For example, even with the best technology, you still need people who can tell a story to sell it. Having said that, I do think some skills are more in demand than others.

**Q: Do you think smart children are happier than other children?** → [OPINION]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| STANCE | 不一定。 | Not necessarily. |
| because | 因为聪明 帮你把事情做好，但它不会自动 让你开心——有时候 反而 压力更大。 | because being smart helps you do well, but it doesn't automatically make you happy — sometimes it just adds more pressure. |
| e.g. | 比如，很多聪明的孩子 背负着 很高的期望，那会 让他们很紧张。 | For example, a lot of smart kids carry really high expectations, and that makes them stressed. |
| LAND | 话虽如此，把一件事做好 确实感觉不错。 | Having said that, doing something well does feel good. |

> Not necessarily. Because being smart helps you do well, but it doesn't automatically make you happy — sometimes it just adds more pressure. For example, a lot of smart kids carry really high expectations, and that makes them stressed. Having said that, doing something well does feel good.

**Q: Is it important for schools to identify and develop each student's talents?** → [OPINION]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| STANCE | 当然，是的。 | Definitely, yes. |
| because | 因为每个孩子 擅长的东西不一样，所以如果学校能早点发现 一个孩子擅长什么，就能帮他 往那个方向发展。 | because every kid is good at different things, so if a school can spot early what a kid is good at, it can help them grow in that direction. |
| e.g. | 比如，一个数学不行 但很会画画的孩子，还是应该有机会 [去]发光。 | For example, a kid who's bad at maths but great at drawing should still get a chance to shine. |
| LAND | 话虽如此，班太大的时候 这真的很难做到。 | Having said that, it's really hard to do when classes are so big. |

> Definitely, yes. Because every kid is good at different things, so if a school can spot early what a kid is good at, it can help them grow in that direction. For example, a kid who's bad at maths but great at drawing should still get a chance to shine. Having said that, it's really hard to do when classes are so big.

## 老14 · A friend who learned something without a teacher (Zhangwei, self-taught ML)　(~118s)

**①拆解 · 英式中文珠子 → floor 英文**

| 拍/模式 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| [HOOK] | 好，所以我想聊的那个朋友 是 张伟，一个人（我认识很多年的）。 | OK so the friend I'd like to talk about is Zhangwei, a guy I've known for years. |
| [CORE · rel-who] | 他在科技行业工作，而他真正突出的那一点 是 他基本上自学了一整个新领域，全靠他自己。 | He works in tech, and what really stands out about him is that he basically taught himself a whole new field on his own. |
| [WISH-BEAT] | 所以他要学的那个东西 是 机器学习，就是整个 AI 那一套，而他得从零开始把它学起来，这样他才能在工作上跟得上。 | So what he had to learn was machine learning, the whole AI thing, and he'd need to pick it up from scratch, so he could keep up at work. |
| [WISH-BEAT · dash-unpack] | 他在这方面完全没有背景——没有课，没有老师，就是每天一点点，外加一些小项目，在旁边（业余）搞的。 | He had no background in it at all — no class, no teacher, just a bit every day plus small projects on the side. |
| [EXPLAIN=LAND] | 而他撑下去的那个原因 是 他的公司正在往 AI 那边转，所以他自己的工作正在他脚下变。 | And the reason he pushed through is that his company was moving into AI, so his own job was changing under him. |
| [EXPLAIN=LAND · so-tag] | 很多人可能就直接放弃了，所以对他来说这真的不是一个爱好——它更像是求生存。 | A lot of people would've just given up, so for him it really wasn't a hobby — it was more like survival. |
| [WEIGH] | 那么，关于有个老师会不会更容易，大多数人会说会，一个老师会让它容易很多。 | Now, on whether it'd be easier with a teacher, most people would say yes, a teacher makes it a lot easier. |
| [WEIGH] | 虽然有些人确实说，自己教自己（这件事）在你脑子里记得更牢。 | Although some people do say that teaching yourself sticks better in your head. |
| [WEIGH] | 但说实话，对我来说，一个老师本来会帮他省下一大堆时间，因为他们能马上回答你的问题。 | But honestly, for me, a teacher would've saved him a ton of time, because they can answer your questions right away. |

**②整段（连起来 shadow）**

> OK so the friend I'd like to talk about is Zhangwei, a guy I've known for years. He works in tech, and what really stands out about him is that he basically taught himself a whole new field on his own. So what he had to learn was machine learning, the whole AI thing, and he'd need to pick it up from scratch, so he could keep up at work. He had no background in it at all — no class, no teacher, just a bit every day plus small projects on the side. And the reason he pushed through is that his company was moving into AI, so his own job was changing under him. A lot of people would've just given up, so for him it really wasn't a hobby — it was more like survival. Now, on whether it'd be easier with a teacher, most people would say yes, a teacher makes it a lot easier. Although some people do say that teaching yourself sticks better in your head. But honestly, for me, a teacher would've saved him a ton of time, because they can answer your questions right away.

**③ P3 follow-ups**

**Q: Is it necessary to keep learning after graduating from school?** → [OPINION]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| STANCE | 绝对的。 | Definitely. |
| because | 因为这世界变得 快得离谱，尤其是技术，所以你在学校学的那些东西 很快就过时了。 | because the world changes so fast, especially technology, so what you learn at school goes out of date quickly. |
| e.g. | 比如，我一个朋友 自学了机器学习——那东西 在他上学的时候 根本还不存在。 | For instance, a friend of mine taught himself machine learning — that stuff didn't even exist when he was at school. |
| LAND | 话虽如此，我不觉得 你非得坐在教室里；边工作边学 也算学。 | Having said that, I don't think you have to sit in a classroom; learning while you work counts too. |

> Definitely. Because the world changes so fast, especially technology, so what you learn at school goes out of date quickly. For instance, a friend of mine taught himself machine learning — that stuff didn't even exist when he was at school. Having said that, I don't think you have to sit in a classroom; learning while you work counts too.

**Q: Should teachers make learning in their classes fun?** → [OPINION]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| STANCE | 某种程度上，是的。 | To some extent, yes. |
| because | 因为如果一堂课 无聊得要死，学生就 走神了，所以他们 记不住多少东西。 | because if a class is really boring, students just zone out, so they don't remember much. |
| e.g. | 比如，我最喜欢的老师 常常用游戏和小故事，那 让(make) 那些枯燥的东西 好记多了。 | For example, my favourite teacher used to use games and little stories, and that made the boring stuff much easier to remember. |
| LAND | 话虽如此，光有趣 不够——他们 还是得 把真东西 教到位。 | Having said that, fun on its own isn't enough — they still have to actually get the real content across. |

> To some extent, yes. Because if a class is really boring, students just zone out, so they don't remember much. For example, my favourite teacher used to use games and little stories, and that made the boring stuff much easier to remember. Having said that, fun on its own isn't enough — they still have to actually get the real content across.

**Q: Do you think there are too many subjects for students to learn?** → [OPINION]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| STANCE | 老实说，有点吧。 | Honestly, a bit, yeah. |
| because | 因为孩子们 一天要塞进 七八门课，所以他们 没时间 把任何一门 学深。 | because kids have to cram seven or eight subjects into a day, so they don't have time to go deep on any one of them. |
| e.g. | 比如，我表弟 每天做作业 到半夜，可他 好像 什么都没真学会。 | For instance, my cousin does homework till midnight every day, but he doesn't seem to really learn anything. |
| LAND | 话虽如此，广一点 也有好处——你 至少 能发现 你喜欢什么。 | Having said that, being broad has its upside — at least you find out what you like. |

> Honestly, a bit, yeah. Because kids have to cram seven or eight subjects into a day, so they don't have time to go deep on any one of them. For instance, my cousin does homework till midnight every day, but he doesn't seem to really learn anything. Having said that, being broad has its upside — at least you find out what you like.

**Q: Is it better to focus on a few subjects or to learn many subjects?** → [COMPARE]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| STANCE | 我觉得 这两种 挺不一样的。 | They're quite different, I reckon. |
| compare | 专注几门 让(make)你 变得很深、很专业，而 学很多门 让你 什么都懂一点。 | Focusing on a few makes you deep and specialised, whereas learning many makes you know a bit of everything. |
| e.g. | 比如，我那个 自学机器学习的 朋友 只死磕一样东西，所以他 现在 真的很厉害。 | For example, my friend who taught himself machine learning just hammered one thing, so now he's really good at it. |
| LAND | 所以 这真的 就看 你想要深度 还是广度。 | So it's really about whether you want depth or breadth. |

> They're quite different, I reckon. Focusing on a few makes you deep and specialised, whereas learning many makes you know a bit of everything. For example, my friend who taught himself machine learning just hammered one thing, so now he's really good at it. So it's really about whether you want depth or breadth.

**Q: Do you think enterprises should provide training for their employees?** → [OPINION]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| STANCE | 绝对的。 | Absolutely. |
| because | 因为如果 公司 帮员工 变得更强，那 公司自己 也跟着 变得更强，所以 这对双方 都好。 | because if a company helps its staff get better, the company gets better too, so it's good for both sides. |
| e.g. | 比如，很多科技公司 给员工 报销 网课，那 让(keep) 大家 一直跟得上 新技术。 | For instance, a lot of tech firms pay for online courses, and that keeps everyone up to date with new tech. |
| LAND | 话虽如此，我不觉得 该 强迫 大家——培训 得是 他们真想要的。 | Having said that, I don't think you should force people — the training has to be something they actually want. |

> Absolutely. Because if a company helps its staff get better, the company gets better too, so it's good for both sides. For instance, a lot of tech firms pay for online courses, and that keeps everyone up to date with new tech. Having said that, I don't think you should force people — the training has to be something they actually want.

**Q: Do you think it is good for older adults to continue learning?** → [OPINION]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| STANCE | 哦，绝对好。 | Oh, definitely. |
| because | 因为 学新东西(这件事) 让(keep) 你的大脑 保持活跃，所以 你 老得 慢一点。 | because learning new things keeps your brain active, so you age a bit more slowly. |
| e.g. | 比如，我奶奶 六十多岁 才学 用智能手机，现在 她 天天 跟我们 视频。 | For example, my grandma learned to use a smartphone in her sixties, and now she video-calls us every day. |
| LAND | 话虽如此，对他们来说 得慢一点——你 不能 硬塞 太多东西 给他们。 | Having said that, it has to be slower for them — you can't cram too much at them. |

> Oh, definitely. Because learning new things keeps your brain active, so you age a bit more slowly. For example, my grandma learned to use a smartphone in her sixties, and now she video-calls us every day. Having said that, it has to be slower for them — you can't cram too much at them.

## 老15 · An event where you didn't enjoy the music played　(~112s)

**①拆解 · 英式中文珠子 → floor 英文**

| 拍/模式 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| [HOOK] | 那我想聊的那个活动 是 一个音乐节（几年前我去过的那个）。 | So the event I'd like to talk about is a music festival I went to a couple of years ago. |
| [CORE] | 它是在户外的、还挺大的，而且说实话，我就是不喜欢它。 | It was outdoors and pretty big, and honestly, I just didn't enjoy it. |
| [FACT-DROP - rel-who] | 我是和几个朋友一起去的（那些真的很迷那种东西的）。 | I went with a few friends who were really into that kind of stuff. |
| [FACT-DROP] | 这是他们的主意，不是我的。 | It was their idea, not mine. |
| [WISH-BEAT] | 他们邀请了我，所以我想我就试一试吧。 | They invited me, so I figured I'd give it a try. |
| [WISH-BEAT - so-tag] | 我不太常去那样的活动，所以我想我就去看看它到底有什么可火的。 | I don't go to things like that very often, so I thought I'd see what the hype was about. |
| [EXPLAIN=LAND] | 我不喜欢它的原因 是 它就是不是我喜欢的那种音乐，因为它是那种特别吵的电子音乐。 | The reason I didn't enjoy it is it just wasn't my kind of music, because it was really loud electronic music. |
| [EXPLAIN=LAND] | 过了一会儿它听起来全都一个样，所以我就有点放空了。 | After a while it all sounded the same, so I kind of switched off. |
| [EXPLAIN=LAND - so-tag] | 而且它还特别挤，所以又热、又很难走动。 | And it was super crowded too, so it was hot and hard to move around. |
| [EXPLAIN=LAND] | 所以我就是那种更喜欢在家过个安静晚上的人，而且对我来说，说实话它结束的时候我还有点松了口气。 | So I'm just more of a quiet-evening-at-home kind of person, and for me, I was honestly a bit relieved when it was over. |

**②整段（连起来 shadow）**

> So the event I'd like to talk about is a music festival I went to a couple of years ago. It was outdoors and pretty big, and honestly, I just didn't enjoy it. I went with a few friends who were really into that kind of stuff. It was their idea, not mine. They invited me, so I figured I'd give it a try. I don't go to things like that very often, so I thought I'd see what the hype was about. The reason I didn't enjoy it is it just wasn't my kind of music, because it was really loud electronic music. After a while it all sounded the same, so I kind of switched off. And it was super crowded too, so it was hot and hard to move around. So I'm just more of a quiet-evening-at-home kind of person, and for me, I was honestly a bit relieved when it was over.

**③ P3 follow-ups**

| because | 我想这主要是因为(because of) 那种气氛——一大群人一起，感觉很嗨。 | I think it's mainly because of the atmosphere — a big crowd together, it feels exciting. |

> Quite a few, actually. The biggest one's music festivals, I'd say. For instance, young people love going to outdoor festivals like Strawberry Festival with their friends. I think it's mainly because of the atmosphere — a big crowd together, it feels exciting.

**Q: Do you think children should receive some musical education?** → [OPINION]

| e.g. | 比如，一个学钢琴的孩子 得 每天坚持练，所以这也教会他们耐心。 | For example, a child who learns piano has to practise every day, so it also teaches them patience. |

> Definitely. Because learning music from a young age helps kids relax and makes them more creative. For example, a child who learns piano has to practise every day, so it also teaches them patience. Having said that, I wouldn't force them if they really don't like it.

**Q: What are the differences between old and young people's music preferences?** → [COMPARE]

| LAND | 所以说到底，真的就是关于 他们是 在什么年代长大的(they grew up in a different time)。 | So it really comes down to what era they grew up in. |

> They're quite different, I reckon. Old people tend to like old songs, slower ones, whereas young people prefer pop and rap, faster stuff. So it really comes down to what era they grew up in.

**Q: What kind of music events are there in your country?** → [ENUMERATE]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|

| e.g. | 比如，很多大明星 会来 大城市 开演唱会，票 经常 秒光。 | For instance, lots of big stars come to big cities to hold concerts, and the tickets often sell out in seconds. |
| 补充 | 除了这个，还有 音乐节 跟 一些小的现场演出、在酒吧里。 | Apart from that, there are also festivals and some small live shows in bars. |

> Quite a few. The most common one's concerts, I'd say. For instance, lots of big stars come to big cities to hold concerts, and the tickets often sell out in seconds. Apart from that, there are also festivals and some small live shows in bars.

**Q: Why do many people like listening to music while doing sports?** → [REASON]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|

| because | 因为 快节奏的音乐 让你 忘掉累，所以你能 坚持更久。 | because fast music makes you forget you're tired, so you can keep going longer. |
| e.g. | 比如，我跑步的时候 总戴着耳机，没有音乐 我很快就想停。 | For example, I always wear earphones when I run, and without music I want to stop pretty quickly. |

> Mainly because it keeps them motivated, I'd say. Because fast music makes you forget you're tired, so you can keep going longer. For example, I always wear earphones when I run, and without music I want to stop pretty quickly.

**Q: What are the differences between listening to music at home and at a live concert?** → [COMPARE]

|---|---|---|

| LAND | 所以说到底，真的就是关于 你想要 安静 还是 那种气氛。 | So it really comes down to whether you want quiet or that atmosphere. |

> They're quite different, I reckon. Listening at home is more relaxed and casual, whereas a live concert is more exciting because you're surrounded by a big crowd. So it really comes down to whether you want quiet or that atmosphere.

## 老16 · A movie you watched and enjoyed recently — 重看星际穿越 (Interstellar)　(~122s)

**①拆解 · 英式中文珠子 → floor 英文**

| 拍/模式 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| [HOOK / which-tag] | OK 那么 我想聊的那部电影 是 星际穿越，我其实最近又重看了一遍。 | OK so the movie I want to talk about is Interstellar, which I actually rewatched recently. |
| [CORE] | 我看它 在家，某个周末，而我爱它的那一点 是 它击中你，每一次都不一样。 | I watched it at home one weekend, and the thing I love about it is it hits you differently each time. |
| [FACT-DROP] | 我看它 和我老婆一起，就我们俩，一个不错的安静的宅家夜晚。 | I watched it with my wife, just the two of us, a nice quiet night in. |
| [PLOT] | 所以它基本上是关于 一群宇航员（那些穿越太空的），去给人类找一个新家。 | So it's basically about a group of astronauts who travel through space to find a new home for people. |
| [PLOT] | 它开始于 地球正在死去，然后他们飞走，去找别的地方住。 | It starts with Earth dying, then they fly off to look for somewhere else to live. |
| [PLOT] | 而酷的那一点是，在所有那些科学的底下，它其实是一个故事，关于一个爸爸和他的女儿。 | And the cool part is, underneath all the science, it's really a story about a dad and his daughter. |
| [EXPLAIN=LAND] | 我又看了它一遍，主要因为我是个大科幻迷，而且我之前看过，但想再感受它一次。 | I watched it again mainly because I'm a big sci-fi fan, and I'd seen it before but wanted to feel it again. |
| [EXPLAIN=LAND] | 第一次，我被那些画面震撼到了，而这一次 那个故事 真的触动了我，因为我现在也是个爸爸了。 | The first time, I was blown away by the visuals, and this time the story actually got to me, because I'm a dad now too. |
| [EXPLAIN=LAND] | 所以对我来说 它既震撼又感人，而那种混合 正是我爱它的原因。 | So for me it's both mind-blowing and touching, and that mix is exactly why I love it. |

**②整段（连起来 shadow）**

> OK so the movie I want to talk about is Interstellar, which I actually rewatched recently. I watched it at home one weekend, and the thing I love about it is it hits you differently each time. I watched it with my wife, just the two of us, a nice quiet night in. So it's basically about a group of astronauts who travel through space to find a new home for people. It starts with Earth dying, then they fly off to look for somewhere else to live. And the cool part is, underneath all the science, it's really a story about a dad and his daughter. I watched it again mainly because I'm a big sci-fi fan, and I'd seen it before but wanted to feel it again. The first time, I was blown away by the visuals, and this time the story actually got to me, because I'm a dad now too. So for me it's both mind-blowing and touching, and that mix is exactly why I love it.

**③ P3 follow-ups**

**Q: What kinds of movies do you think are successful in your country?** → [ENUMERATE]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|

| e.g. | 像超级英雄片、或者那种动作大片，在中国总是卖得特别好。 | Like superhero films or big action movies always sell really well in China. |
| because | 因为人们去电影院、就是想要那种视觉冲击（你在家 得不到的）。 | because people go to the cinema wanting the kind of visual punch they can't get at home. |

> The biggest one's big special-effects blockbusters, I'd say. Like superhero films or big action movies always sell really well in China. Because people go to the cinema wanting the kind of visual punch they can't get at home.

**Q: What are the factors that make a successful movie?** → [ENUMERATE]

|---|---|---|

| 命名最大 | 但我会说，主要的一个 就是一个好故事。 | But the main one's a good story, I'd say. |
| because | 因为就算那特效 再花哨，要是那剧情 站不住脚，人们就会觉得无聊。 | because even if the effects are flashy, if the story doesn't hold up, people just get bored. |

> Quite a few, actually. But the main one's a good story, I'd say. Because even if the effects are flashy, if the story doesn't hold up, people just get bored.

**Q: Do Chinese people prefer to watch domestic movies or foreign movies?** → [COMPARE]

|---|---|---|

| 咬差异 | 国产片讲的是 我们自己的文化、所以更贴近人心，而外国片 主要就图个新鲜。 | Domestic films are about our own culture, so they hit closer to home, whereas foreign ones are mostly just for something different. |
| LAND | 所以说到底，这真的就看 你想要熟悉感、还是新鲜感。 | So it really comes down to whether you want something familiar or something new. |

> These days, I'd say most people actually prefer domestic ones now. Domestic films are about our own culture, so they hit closer to home, whereas foreign ones are mostly just for something different. So it really comes down to whether you want something familiar or something new.

**Q: Do you think only well-known directors can create the best movies?** → [OPINION]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|

| because | 因为一部好电影靠的是 一个好点子和团队合作，而不只是 那个大牌名字。 | because a good film comes down to a good idea and teamwork, not just the big name. |
| e.g. | 其实，很多新导演 拍出了很棒的片子、还拿了奖。 | In fact, loads of new directors have made brilliant films and even won awards. |
| LAND | 话虽如此，有名的导演确实 更容易拿到钱和资源。 | Having said that, well-known directors do find it easier to get money and resources. |

> Not necessarily. Because a good film comes down to a good idea and teamwork, not just the big name. In fact, loads of new directors have made brilliant films and even won awards. Having said that, well-known directors do find it easier to get money and resources.

**Q: Do you think successful movies should have well-known actors or actresses in leading roles?** → [OPINION]

| 拍 | 英式中文珠子 | floor 英文 |
|---|---|---|
| STANCE | 某种程度上，是的。 | To some extent, yes. |
| because | 因为大牌明星 能吸引粉丝、也能卖出票。 | because big stars pull in fans and sell tickets. |
| LAND | 话虽如此，要是那故事很烂，就算一个大明星 也救不了它。 | Having said that, if the story is bad, even a big star can't save it. |

> To some extent, yes. Because big stars pull in fans and sell tickets. Having said that, if the story is bad, even a big star can't save it.

**Q: Why do people prefer to watch movies in the cinema?** → [REASON]

|---|---|---|
